scratchy — known gaps / deferred work

Plain checklist of things found-and-deliberately-deferred during work on
this project, so they don't get forgotten between sessions. Not a
feature backlog of things nobody asked for — only things we actually
hit and chose not to fix immediately. See NOTES.md for the fuller story
behind any item; a commit hash in parens points at the commit it came
up during (`git show <hash>`) — more precise than a PROMPTS.md version
number, since several versions can share one commit and a hash is
directly actionable. PROMPTS.md is append-only and never edited for
these.

========================================================================
QUEUE — ranked work order (reviewed 2026-11-12, re-planned 2026-11-14)
========================================================================

- Agents never `cargo install`; the coordinator installs from main.

Work through this queue top to bottom; it overrides the order of
everything below it. Everything below the QUEUE is history and detail.
An item here that says "Detail:" points at the longer entry further
down by its title text. Search for that text, because line numbers in
this file go stale.

Rules for whoever implements an item:
- Take the first unchecked item. Don't reorder the queue and don't
  merge items. If an item turns out to be wrong or much bigger than its
  size suggests, stop and report back. Don't guess.
- Follow the per-item checklist in `AGENTS.md`.
- Code references are function and type names only. Find them with
  `grep -rn 'fn <name>' src/`, and never trust a line number.
- When an item is done, tick it here AND in its "Detail:" entry, with
  the commit hash. If you deliberately leave part of an item out, add a
  new, narrower `[ ]` item right below it in this QUEUE.
- Sizes: S = under ~200 changed lines, a single concept. M = several
  functions, new plumbing. L = split it into the listed sub-items and
  do them one at a time.

Evidence base: `SCRATCHY_CSS_DEBUG=1` runs plus raw-CSS selector and
at-rule counts on four pages the user actually reads: Wikipedia
(SoHo_Weekly_News), social.loupax.com/u/loupax, forums.gentoo.org and
google.com. Numbers like "(n=47)" in an item are ignored declarations or
dropped selectors summed across those pages. Caveats:
- The gentoo page is CSS-only. `snag` timed out mid-fetch twice (its own
  bug, not scratchy's), so the page body never arrived.
- The debug tool under-reports. It reports "no change" when a rule had
  already set the same value (Wikipedia writes every color twice,
  `color:#202122; color:var(--color-base,#202122)`). It never reports
  selectors or at-rules that were dropped wholesale. Q1 fixes both of
  those blind spots.
- Most of the sites the user browses require JavaScript, so they can't
  be measured yet. Re-run this measurement after Tier 1 lands, and
  re-rank if the numbers move.

--- YOUTUBE CPU BUDGET + SOCIAL VIM.JS (Opus investigator, 2026-11-16) -------
--- Evidence: REGRESSIONS.md R24 (YouTube) and R25 (social.loupax.com). ------
--- All times are thread-CPU ms from a TEMPORARY local instrumentation of -----
--- `JsEngine::eval` (compile-only JS_Eval, then JS_EvalFunction; every -------
--- Rust `__*` native wrapped in a JS timer); none of it was committed. -------

YouTube (kevlar_base variant, 10578 KB; youtube serves it about 2 times out
of 3, the rest get `base_module_set`, 1184 KB, which loads in 0.2 s and
dies on an unrelated `Symbol.iterator of undefined` at m=base:1934):
compile 0.92-1.42 s, run 3.62 s before the trip (host 3.04 s, JS 0.59 s).
Host = `__d_query` alone: 751 calls, 3027 ms. The budget is PER SCRIPT
(`arm_budget` in every `eval`), but it is armed BEFORE compiling, so
compile eats 20-28% of it. With the budget raised locally to 120 s the
script finishes after 12.7 s of run (host 11.2 s = `__d_query` 1646 calls
11.1 s; JS 1.6 s). Preludes cost ~45 ms compile + ~5 ms run per page;
`getComputedStyle` 67 calls 7.6 ms; no relayout is ever triggered (JS has
no geometry). So: not the engine, not our hooks in general, ONE hook.

- [x] QM1 [Opus, investigate only] (user request 2026-11-17) Can scratchy EMBED an
      already installed external video player (mpv or similar) instead of decoding
      media itself? Survey: mpv `--wid` / libmpv render API (Wayland: no X11 window
      id; subsurface/dmabuf options), mpv IPC socket control, a separate Wayland
      toplevel placed over the video box vs a real subsurface, vlc/ffplay/gstreamer
      alternatives, how to map `<video>` geometry/scroll/clipping/z-order to the
      player, audio routing, what the HTMLMediaElement JS surface would need
      (play/pause/currentTime/events), security (player given a URL: wire's request
      policy, no direct network from the player, or a local pipe), cost/size of each
      option and what a minimal spike would look like. PRODUCT DESIGN STAYS WITH THE
      USER (AGENTS.md Off limits: media waits for the agreed design); this item only
      writes the findings and recommendations into TODO.txt/NOTES.md. No source edits.
      DONE 2026-11-17 (Opus, docs only). Evidence and measurements: REGRESSIONS.md
      R27. Short version:
      ANSWER: an mpv PROCESS cannot be embedded in scratchy's window on Wayland.
      `--wid` is X11-only (this mpv is built with x11=disabled; tested: with
      `--wid=12345` it still opened its own xdg_toplevel), and Wayland has no
      cross-client embedding: a wl_subsurface can only be made by the client that
      owns the parent surface (dwl has no xdg-foreign either, which would only
      parent toplevels anyway). A second toplevel is tiled by dwl (tested in a
      nested dwl: mpv took the whole output), cannot place itself, and would not
      follow scroll. What DOES work is mpv as a LIBRARY in scratchy's process:
      libmpv 2.5 is installed (/usr/lib64/libmpv.so.2.5.0, 2.4 MB, headers
      client.h, render.h, render_gl.h, stream_cb.h). Its render API draws each frame
      into memory WE own (MPV_RENDER_API_TYPE_SW: rgb0/bgr0 + stride + pointer) or
      into a GL FBO, its stream_cb API lets us be the ONLY byte source
      (`scratchy-media://<id>` with read/seek/size callbacks), and its client API
      gives the same commands/properties/events as the IPC socket (tested over
      IPC: loadfile, duration 20.0, width/height, seekable, pause, seek to 12.5 ->
      time-pos 12.5, volume, per-frame time-pos events, file-loaded/
      playback-restart/seek events).
      RECOMMENDATION (for the user to accept or change): option A1 = libmpv
      in-process, software render of each frame into a scratchy buffer the size of
      the `<video>` box, painted like an `<img>` whose pixels change. Geometry,
      scroll, overflow clipping, z-order (page content over the video) and QY18
      translate all come from the existing paint path for free. All bytes come
      from wire through stream_cb (policy, cookie jar, CORS kept; mpv's own
      network unused). Plain `<video src>` / `<source>` with mp4 (h264/hevc/aac)
      and webm (vp8/vp9/av1 + opus/vorbis) is realistic: ffmpeg here decodes all
      of them. MediaSource (MSE) and EME are NOT realistic: YouTube, Twitch and
      most big video sites need MSE, so they stay without video. Cost measured
      here (Ryzen 5 PRO 2500U, software decode only: this mpv/ffmpeg has no
      VA-API): 720p30 h264 ~24% of one core, 1080p30 ~43%; software scaling to a
      ~1048x589 shm buffer (vo=wlshm, the closest CLI proxy for SW render) added
      ~23% of a core at 720p; mpv RSS 117 MB / 23 threads while playing 1080p.
      Today `App::redraw` repaints the whole page and presents without damage on
      every frame, so 30 fps video needs a video-rect-only redraw path (QM1c).
      Rejected or later: A2 (own wl_subsurface + wp_viewporter: compositor scales,
      but clipping and page content above the video become hard), B (libmpv
      OpenGL: needs an EGL context scratchy does not have; later optimisation),
      C (spawn mpv as its own tiled window: not embedding, but a cheap "open this
      video in mpv" action if the user wants one), D (ffmpeg/ffplay subprocess
      writing raw frames: we would rebuild A/V sync and audio; ffplay has no
      control protocol), E (vlc, gstreamer: not installed; GStreamer waylandsink
      has the same in-process constraint and is a heavier dependency).
      Children (WAITING FOR USER unless marked READY):
- [ ] QM1-D WAITING FOR USER (design first; media is "Off limits" in AGENTS.md)
      product questions, answers gate everything below except QM1a/QM1c/QM1d:
      D1 In-page video (A1) at all, or first only a "open in mpv" action on the
         placeholder (C: own dwl tile, mpv does its own networking unless D4 says
         otherwise)? Or both, C first because it is a day of work?
      D2 libmpv inside scratchy's process (simplest; ffmpeg parses untrusted media
         next to the JS engine and page state) or a separate media helper process
         that renders frames into shared memory (memfd) for scratchy (isolation,
         about 2x the work)?
      D3 Hard dependency on libmpv, or optional (cargo feature or dlopen at run
         time; without it `<video>` stays the inert placeholder)? Recommended:
         optional, so scratchy still builds and runs without mpv.
      D4 All media bytes through wire (recommended: same policy, cookies, CORS as
         everything else; needs a Range-capable media channel, QM1d/QM1e), or may
         the player fetch directly (bypasses wire's policy and jar)?
      D5 Autoplay: never / muted only / allowed. D6 Controls: who draws them
         (scratchy paints a simple bar; mpv's OSC is a Lua script and would be off),
         which keys (vim-mode conflicts: space, j/k, arrows). D7 `<audio>` too
         (same backend, no video), and how many players at once (each ~100 MB RSS;
         suggest 2, pause the oldest). D8 Accept "no MSE/EME" (YouTube stays
         without video), or ask for a separate MSE investigation later.

- [x] QY21 [S] (959cd39) (P2, YouTube load wall) QY6e `define()` is quadratic. Root
      cause: `CER.prototype.define` (js_dom.rs, QY6e f887f67) rebuilds
      `ceSel` = every defined name joined with ',' and runs
      `__d_query(0, ceSel)` over the whole document on EVERY define;
      `ceScan`/`ceCollect` (run on every hooked insert/remove) use the same
      ever-growing list. `__d_query` re-parses the list and matches every
      element against every name: O(defines^2 x elements). YouTube defines
      ~700 elements over 1227-1318 elements: 35 ms per late define.
      Fix: (1) `define` queries only the new name (`ext + '[is]'` for a
      customized built-in) - measured locally: kevlar run 12.7 s -> 1.55 s
      (host 11.2 s -> 0.41 s), compile+run 2.9 s, the 5 s load error is GONE;
      (2) `ceScan`/`ceCollect`: replace the selector with a native
      `__d_ce_candidates(root)` that walks `root` once and keeps elements
      whose local name (or `is` attribute) is in a Rust `HashSet` the
      prelude updates on define (the remaining 325 ceScan calls cost 227 ms
      at load and ~500 ms more in the first 5 s of timers). Do NOT cache
      by selector string (the string changes on every define).
      Test: define 600 names over a 1500-element document and insert 300
      subtrees; assert `__d_query` is called O(defines) times with
      one-name selectors (count via a test hook) and that upgrade order /
      connectedCallback order is unchanged (existing QY6e tests pass).
      Accept: `--js-report` on a saved kevlar_base youtube copy (R24
      recipe) has no "exceeded the 5 s" line. Unblocks: YouTube load; any
      Polymer/Lit page that defines many elements.
- [ ] QY22 [S] (P2) Budget policy fixes, all measured on YouTube:
      (a) arm the page-load budget AFTER compiling: compile with
      `JS_EVAL_FLAG_COMPILE_ONLY`, then `JS_EvalFunction` (exactly what the
      R24 instrumentation did; it ran fine), and log compile time apart in
      the interrupt message (`compiled in 1.4 s, ran 3.6 s`). Compile is
      bounded by source size (1.4 s per 10 MB here), not by page behaviour,
      so it should not decide whether the script may run; keep a hard cap
      (e.g. stop compiling sources > 32 MB with a clear log line) instead.
      (b) the CASCADE: after a timer/event callback trips, `drain_jobs`
      keeps executing the queued promise jobs under the already-expired
      deadline, so each dies at once: a live window run logged 6684
      `Uncaught (in promise job) InternalError: interrupted` lines in 40 s
      (then QuickJS `out of memory` at the 256 MB limit, 4 times). Fix: one
      log line per tripped drain (count the dropped jobs), and give
      microtasks drained after a task one fresh CALLBACK_BUDGET of their
      own instead of 0 ms. (c) keep CALLBACK_BUDGET = 500 ms (R8), but note
      YouTube's two biggest timer callbacks measured 465 and 491 ms with
      QY21(1) applied: re-measure after QY21(2) (QY27).
      Test: a script whose promise chain of 1000 jobs follows a 600 ms busy
      loop logs ONE interrupt line, not 1000; a 3 s compile-equivalent
      script is not counted against the 5 s run budget (unit-test the
      split eval with a fake clock). Unblocks: every big-bundle site.
- [ ] QY23 [S] (P2, YouTube next error after QY21) `element.style = "..."`
      throws `TypeError: no setter for property` in strict code (our
      `style` accessor is getter-only; spec: [PutForwards=cssText]).
      Polymer's `_attributeToProperty` (kevlar :8885:184) assigns
      `this.style = <string>` from attributeChangedCallback. Fix: setter
      that writes cssText (null -> ''). Test: `"use strict"; d.style =
      'color: red'` sets the attribute and `d.style.color === 'red'`.
- [ ] QY24 [S] (P2, YouTube) `classList` dies with `cannot read property
      'split' of undefined` (prelude `classes`, called from kevlar
      `RHo`): `classes(el)` reads `el.className`, a property pages and
      frameworks shadow (ShadyDOM/Polymer elements); read the `class`
      attribute through `__d_attr` instead (SVG `className` is a string
      here today, so that is not the cause). Test: a custom element class
      with `get className(){ return undefined; }` still supports
      `classList.add/contains/toggle`.
- [ ] QY25 [S] (P3, diagnostics) Custom element and MutationObserver
      callback errors lose the message: `ceReport` (js_dom.rs) prints only
      `e.stack`, and a QuickJS stack has no `Name: message` line, so the
      user sees `uncaught exception in custom element callback:     at
      <anonymous> ...`. Print `String(e)` then the stack (the R24 run did
      exactly this to find QY23). Also the callback name is empty for
      minified code: print the element's tag. Test: a connectedCallback
      that throws `new TypeError('boom')` logs a line containing
      `TypeError: boom` and the tag name.
- [ ] QY26 [M] (P4, measured, optional) In-memory bytecode cache for big
      scripts (key: final URL + content hash; session lifetime, no disk:
      QF-D7). Measured on kevlar_base: `JS_WriteObject` 79 ms, the
      bytecode is 32339 KB (3x the source), `JS_ReadObject` 79 ms and the
      read-back function ran with 0 errors, vs 0.92-1.42 s to compile. So
      a reload or revisit saves ~1.3 s per 10 MB, at 32 MB RAM per cached
      script: cap the cache (e.g. 64 MB, LRU). QuickJS has no lazy
      function compilation, so there is no cheaper first-load option short
      of changing engines. Do only after QY21/QY22, and only if the user
      reloads YouTube often.

      `--js-report --run-timers 5000` recipe), incl. peak memory (the
      window run hit `out of memory` at 256 MB, maybe only because of the
      QY22(b) cascade). With QY21(1) and a 60 s callback budget locally:
      548 timer callbacks in 5 s simulated, 96 requests (POST
      /youtubei/v1/guide 200, ~80 icon SVGs, 6 googlevideo `videoplayback`
      GETs: player prefetch through fetch(), not our media element), and
      the window STILL shows only the static grey skeleton. Remaining

      CORS: no Access-Control-Allow-Origin header` (initiator not found;
      the app's beacons use `mode: "no-cors"` which must not be
      CORS-checked: verify which request this is). File the next walls.

templates: egg.js (deferred, every page, guest or logged in, same scripts

localStorage is memory-only, so vim.js always loads and `:set novim` cannot
stick across loads. The ONE js error today: vim.js:104:63 `not a function`
= `Element.getBoundingClientRect` does not exist (local repro: also
`getClientRects`, `offset*`, `client*`, `scroll*`, documentElement
`scrollHeight`, `scrollIntoView`, `Element.scroll/scrollTo/scrollBy`,

- [x] QJ4 [M] (DONE 2026-11-17, commit 674231c; src/js_geom.rs forced
      layout on the JS thread cached per DOM write generation, App scroll
      mirrored into window.scrollY. Left out: images laid out without decoded
      size, inline elements = descendant union/nearest box, element
      scrollTop/Left only remembered (QJ6), no nested-scroller/sticky
      adjustment; /discover now stops at vim.js:260 scrollIntoView = QJ5.)
      (P2, first slice of QY-IO-GEOMETRY, no IO/RO) Geometry read
      API: `getBoundingClientRect`/`getClientRects` (DOMRect/DOMRectList),
      `offsetTop/Left/Width/Height/Parent`, `clientTop/Left/Width/Height`,
      `scrollWidth/Height`, `scrollTop/Left` (getter AND setter: vim.js
      does `helpEl.scrollTop = 0`; with a getter-only stand-in that threw
      `no setter for property` at vim.js:1083), on documentElement too
      (`scrollHeight` = page height, `clientHeight` = viewport).
      DESIGN POINT (differs from QY-IO-GEOMETRY's "stale cache"): vim.js
      measures DURING LOAD, before the App has laid the page out. A cache
      filled only after layout would give all-zero rects at that moment,
      so `buildGutter` makes 0 rows (`while (y < pageBottom)` with
      pageBottom 0) and nothing fixes it until a resize. Browsers force a
      synchronous layout on these reads; do the same: a read with a dirty
      geometry generation runs style+layout for the current DOM at the
      current viewport (also in `--js-report`, which today never lays out)
      and caches until the next DOM write. Measure the cost on Wikipedia
      (R3: layout ~1 s there) and on /discover; if a page reads geometry
      in a write/read loop, log once per script ("N forced layouts, M ms").
      Test: a 3-article fixture: tops strictly increase, height > 0,
      documentElement.scrollHeight >= sum of heights, `scrollTop = 0`
      does not throw. Accept: /discover `--js-report` past vim.js:104;
      clamp.js no longer clamps short posts (today `b.scrollHeight` is
      undefined, so `undefined <= n` is false and ALL 17/17 posts get
      `max-height: 210px` + a "Read more" button: seen in the window).
      Unblocks: social.loupax.com (vim.js gutter, clamp.js), lists.js
      move, and any page that measures itself.

- [x] QJ11 DECIDED 2026-11-17 (user): pages in general may override the user-agent
      defaults, so a page may cancel ANY key; scratchy keeps no reserved chord. The
      user turns vim mode off on social.loupax.com for themself. Nothing to build.
      Original analysis (kept for reference):
      Page key handlers vs scratchy's
      own keys. `js_key` (app.rs) gives the page every keydown first and
      lets it cancel; only scratchy's own `:` command-line mode is exempt.
      Once QJ4-QJ6 land, vim.js (document keydown, vim.js:1213) cancels
      `:` `/` h j k l g G n N w b e 0 ^ $ { } * # Enter Esc and Ctrl-D/U/O/I,
      so on social.loupax.com scratchy's `:` command line, `:go`, `:q` and
      its own vim scrolling are unreachable. Also the page's own `:` line
      is an `<input>`, and typing into inputs is forms-off-limits, so
      `:Tag` cannot be TYPED (clicking the `:tag` link at the top of the
      page opens the same picker without typing, and j/k/Enter/Esc work
      in it). Options: (a) a reserved chord the page never sees (e.g.
      Ctrl-`:` or Esc Esc) that opens scratchy's command line; (b) a
      per-site "page keys off" toggle; (c) scratchy wins for a fixed key
      set (breaks vim.js by design). Recommendation: (a).

--- DESIGN MEMO (QSD): Shadow DOM, <template> content, GitHub's module wall --
--- (Opus investigator, 2026-11-16; supersedes the QY6 memo's "no measured --
--- user for native Shadow DOM") -------------------------------------------

This block is now FIRST in the queue. Items marked READY need no
decision and come before everything else (including the QF block below).
Items marked "WAITING FOR USER (design first)" are skipped until the user
ticks the matching SD-D box and writes the chosen option next to it.
Evidence for every number here: R23 in REGRESSIONS.md.

What was measured (release build of `5ca5619`, `--js-report --run-timers
5000` on curl'd pages + `<base href>`, then COPIES with JS stand-ins):
- The github "appendChild of undefined" that kills core/marketing-
  essentials/landing-pages (and 4 module entries on a repo page) is NOT
  shadow DOM. f18-10a827ef5e40729a.js:223:27350 is the task-lists element
  module's top level: `let f=document.createElement("template"); ...
  f.content.appendChild(p)`. `template.content` is undefined (QY6f).
  youtube kevlar_base line 10725:29 is the same API:
  `document.head.appendChild(_.GZ().content)` (a Polymer `<template>`).
  So QY6f alone unblocks both. (YouTube then hits the 5 s CPU page-load
  budget in the 9.5 MB app script: a separate, non-shadow wall.)
- With template.content stood in, github's NEXT error was a scratchy bug:
  GitHub's popover polyfill redefines
  `DocumentFragment.prototype.querySelectorAll` with
  `Object.defineProperty(..., {value})` (= read-only), and scratchy's
  `wrap()` then ASSIGNS `querySelectorAll` onto every fragment wrapper
  (`queries(o)` per instance), which throws `'querySelectorAll' is
  read-only` in strict mode on the first `template.content` access (QSD0).
- The 13 `attachShadow` errors are 12 `<relative-time>` constructors and
  one `<tool-tip>` connectedCallback (f18 136:687). Feature detection
  does not help: relative-time falls back to `this` if `attachShadow` is
  absent, but GitHub's `command` polyfill (f18 ~275321) wraps
  `HTMLElement.prototype.attachShadow` unconditionally, so an absent
  method becomes `undefined.call` -> TypeError.
- Ordered minimal set that runs github.com's 3 module entries and all
  relative-time/tool-tip elements with ZERO startup errors (29 -> 1):
  1 template.content (+ template innerHTML/cloneNode), 2 the QSD0 fix,
  3 attachShadow returning a ShadowRoot + `shadowRoot` getter, 4
  `document.domain`, `document.firstElementChild`, `document.
  getElementsByName` (non-shadow DOM gaps the modules hit next), then
  observed but non-fatal: `new CSSStyleSheet` + `replaceSync` +
  `document.adoptedStyleSheets=`, shadow-root `adoptedStyleSheets=`,
  `getRootNode`, `isConnected`. The one remaining error is
  `crypto.randomUUID` (not a function) in react-core's `react-app`
  connectedCallback, plus one timer over the 500 ms CPU budget and the
  collector POST blocked by CORS preflight (QF13).
- VISIBLE gain on github.com from all of the above: none measurable.
  Every `<relative-time>` on the logged-out home page has NO `datetime`
  attribute (`<relative-time no-title data-component="RelativeTime">Nov
  16, 2026</relative-time>`); its `update()` then copies the light text
  into the shadow root, so Chrome shows the same "Nov 16, 2026" scratchy
  shows today. The tool-tip's shadow root is `<style>` + `<slot>`. The
  real payoff is that github's module code runs at all (React islands,
  menus, the repo page's code view), and its next walls are non-shadow
  (crypto, run-time module imports QY15a: `primer-react-*.js` on a repo
  page). A repo page's file list/dates are React-rendered, not measured.
- Survey (14 pages, R23 table): shadow DOM needed at startup by github
  (JS, imperative), chromestatus.com (whole UI is one Lit app in shadow
  roots: blank without real rendering), developer.mozilla.org (Lit +
  DECLARATIVE shadow DOM: 17 `<template shadowrootmode>` hosts; only the
  "Search" button label and dropdown/theme widgets live in shadow roots;
  `--no-js` screenshot already readable), salesforce.com (185 DSD
  templates, 315 `<slot>`s, 36% of body text inside DSD, incl. the whole
  nav; scratchy's `--no-js` window was blank after 20 s, cause not
  isolated). youtube uses ShadyDOM (`ShadyDOM={force:true}`, no native
  shadow DOM). ddg ships its own fallback (`if(!attachShadow)
  attachShadow=()=>appendChild(div)`, a flattened polyfill) that never
  runs because scratchy defines a throwing attachShadow. bbc only
  feature-detects it for the video player (media: off limits). guardian,
  lobste.rs, wikipedia, HN, web.dev: no use. reddit/old.reddit: www
  returns an 8 KB bot wall to curl; the old.reddit login page saved by
  R22 has shreddit/Lit code but no startup error from it.
- html5ever 0.39 already implements the declarative-shadow-DOM parser
  hook (`should_attach_declarative_shadow`/`attach_declarative_shadow`
  in tree_builder); markup5ever_rcdom's sink returns false, so DSD
  templates parse as ordinary inert templates today (content hidden,
  light children shown). The `selectors` crate already has `:host`,
  `::slotted()`, `::part()` matching behind `Parser::parse_host/
  parse_slotted/parse_part` and `Element::containing_shadow_host/
  parent_node_is_shadow_root/assigned_slot` (all stubbed `false/None` in
  dom.rs `impl SelectorsElement for El`). scratchy has NO
  `display:contents` (needed for `<slot>` and `:host{display:contents}`).

Options:
A) Keep attachShadow throwing (today). Cost 0. GitHub: relative-time and
   tool-tip constructors keep throwing, but after QY6f+QSD0 the module
   entries still die only if something else calls attachShadow at module
   level (none measured), so most of github's JS runs anyway. chromestatus
   stays blank, MDN/salesforce keep today's (DSD-less) rendering.
B) Lightweight composed tree, in two stages.
   B1 "real DOM, not rendered": attachShadow returns a real ShadowRoot
   node (a DocumentFragment-kind node registered on its host in a Rust
   side table), with the JS API (host, mode, shadowRoot getter for open,
   innerHTML, querySelector scoped to the shadow tree, getRootNode,
   isConnected through the host, custom-element callbacks inside it,
   events climbing from shadow root to host). Rendering ignores shadow
   roots: the host's LIGHT children render exactly as today, and a
   one-time log line says shadow content is not rendered. Cost: M (QSD3)
   + S (QSD2). Gain: github relative-time/tool-tip run (measured), Lit
   apps (chromestatus, MDN) stop throwing; nothing new on screen.
   Risk: a component that hides its light children and shows only shadow
   content shows the light children (same as today when it throws).
   B2 "flattened rendering": layout/paint walk a composed tree:
   `composed_children(h)` = the shadow root's children if `h` hosts one,
   a `<slot>`'s assigned light nodes (by `slot=` name, default slot gets
   the rest, fallback content if none) if `h` is a slot in a shadow tree,
   else `h.children`. `<slot>` is a `display:contents` box, so
   `slot[name=x]{display:none}` hides its slotted nodes (MDN's dropdown
   does exactly that). Style: rules from `<style>`/`<link>`/adopted
   sheets inside a shadow root match only that tree (+ `:host`,
   `:host()`, `::slotted()` via the selectors crate hooks); document
   rules do not match into shadow trees; inheritance follows the
   composed tree (free: the layout walk passes `inherited` down).
   Hit-test/hover/focus-within use the composed parent; events retarget
   at the shadow boundary. Cost: display:contents (QSD5, M) + composed
   walk (QSD6, M) + scoping (QSD7, M) + retargeting (QSD8, S) + DSD
   (QSD9, M) ~ 4-5 M items. Gain: MDN's DSD widgets + salesforce's nav
   render; chromestatus can render once fetch works; github's
   relative-time renders through its shadow root (same text).
C) Faithful implementation: B2 plus slotchange/re-slotting on mutation,
   nested slot re-distribution, `::part`/`exportparts`, `:host-context`,
   closed-mode leak proofing, `delegatesFocus` + sequential focus
   navigation across trees, selection across boundaries, `relatedTarget`
   retargeting, `ElementInternals` form association (forms are off
   limits), per-tree `document.activeElement` retargeting. Honest size:
   L (another 5-8 M items on top of B2); no surveyed page needs any of it
   at startup.

[STATUS 2026-11-16 audit: QY6f, QSD0, QSD1, QSD1b, QSD2, QSD4 DONE; QSD5
is the remaining READY one. SD-D1..SD-D4 still unanswered.]
RECOMMENDATION: do the READY items now (QY6f, QSD0, QSD1, QSD2, QSD4,
QSD5); they are correct-by-spec, unblock github's module entries and
youtube's first wall, and need no product decision. Then B1 (QSD3) as
soon as the user OKs it: it is the cheapest honest way to stop 13 github
errors and let Lit apps start, and it renders exactly what scratchy
renders today. Then B2 (QSD6-QSD9) in order, re-measured after each, with
MDN + salesforce + chromestatus as the test pages (github gains nothing
visible from it). Do not do C until a page the user reads needs a
specific piece; file it as narrow items then. Declarative shadow DOM
(QSD9) only after QSD6/QSD7, because a shadow root nobody renders turns
DSD content from "hidden template" into "hidden shadow root" with no
gain.

User decisions:
- [ ] SD-D1 (USER) Is B1 acceptable as a first step: `attachShadow`
      works and the JS sees a real shadow tree, but scratchy keeps
      rendering the host's light children and NOT the shadow content
      (logged once per page as `not implemented: shadow root rendering`)?
      (Recommended: yes, with B2 queued right behind it.) Or A (keep
      throwing until B2 is ready), or go straight to B2.
- [ ] SD-D2 (USER) Event retargeting fidelity. (Recommended) basic:
      composed events (all UA input events, `composed:true` custom
      events) continue from a shadow root to its host; `event.target`
      is retargeted to the host for listeners outside the shadow tree;
      `composedPath()` omits nodes inside closed roots; non-composed
      events stop at the shadow root. Cut: `relatedTarget` retargeting,
      focus/blur retargeting, `delegatesFocus`.
- [ ] SD-D3 (USER) Declarative shadow DOM (`<template shadowrootmode>`,
      parsed by html5ever via a sink wrapper). (Recommended) yes, but
      only after QSD6/QSD7 render shadow trees.
- [ ] SD-D4 (USER) Style scoping scope for B2. (Recommended) `<style>`,
      `<link rel=stylesheet>` and `adoptedStyleSheets` per shadow root,
      `:host`, `:host(<sel>)`, `::slotted(<compound>)`; CUT `::part`/
      `exportparts` and `:host-context` (parsed and dropped, logged).

Items (in order):
- [x] QSD0 [S] (824954b) READY Fragment wrappers must not assign query methods per
      instance. `wrap()` in js_dom.rs calls `queries(o)` for every
      kind-11 node, i.e. `o.querySelectorAll = ...` on the instance; once
      a page has made `DocumentFragment.prototype.querySelectorAll`
      read-only (GitHub's popover polyfill: `Object.defineProperty(
      DocumentFragment.prototype, "querySelectorAll", {value})`), the
      assignment throws `'querySelectorAll' is read-only` in strict mode.
      Fix: call `queries(DocumentFragment.prototype)` once (like
      `queries(ElementProto)`) and drop the per-instance call. Test: after
      `Object.defineProperty(DocumentFragment.prototype,
      'querySelectorAll', {value: function(){return []}})`,
      `document.createDocumentFragment()` does not throw and uses the
      page's method. Payoff: required before QY6f is useful on github.
- [x] QY6f [M] (ac492ca) READY (moved here from the QY6 block; Detail: search
      "QY6f [M] `<template>` content") `template.content`. Additions from

--- DESIGN MEMO (QF): fetch / XMLHttpRequest / networking from page JS ----
--- (Opus investigator, 2026-11-16; replaces the Q33f entries) ----------

This block is next in the queue. It splits Q33f into QF1-QF19. The
items marked READY implement the most restrictive defaults below and
need no decision. The items marked "WAITING FOR USER (design first)"
must be skipped until the user ticks the matching QF-D box and writes
the chosen option next to it. Evidence for every number here: R22 in
REGRESSIONS.md.

What the survey found (curl'd pages + `<base href>`, `--js-report
--run-timers 5000`, every network API replaced by a logging stand-in):
- Startup traffic is small and read-only. guardian: 1 data GET
  (`api.nextgen.guardianapps.co.uk/geolocation`, JSON, cross-origin,
  `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`) plus 9 analytics GETs to
  `ophan.theguardian.com` (`mode:'no-cors'`, `keepalive`,
  `credentials:'include'`). bbc: 1 GET `www.bbc.co.uk/userinfo` (JSON,
  cross-origin, ACAO echoes the origin). duckduckgo: 1 same-origin GET
  `/country.json` and 4 `navigator.sendBeacon` calls. old.reddit (served
  as the login page): 1 GET `www.reddit.com/svc/events/preload-head` (no
  ACAO, so Chrome cannot read it either). HN and lobste.rs: none at
  startup. github and youtube die before their first request
  (customElements / MutationObserver, QY6); youtube's data API
  ("innertube") is POST with a JSON body (static read of the 9.8 MB app
  script; R21 saw it call `/youtubei/v1/player` plus 336 icon GETs).
- Not one surveyed page used `XMLHttpRequest`, `FormData`, `Blob`,
  `response.body.getReader()`, `EventSource`, `WebSocket` or sync XHR at
  startup. Every network call went through `fetch`, and every response
  was read with `.json()`.
- guardian's 7 (+2 in timers) `ActiveXObject is not defined` errors
  come from polyfill.io's fetch polyfill, which looks for
  `XMLHttpRequest` and then `ActiveXObject`. With either `fetch` or
  XHR present: 0 errors. polyfill.io also installs a SYNCHRONOUS XHR POST
  as its `sendBeacon` fallback, so `sendBeacon` must exist.
- github: `TextEncoder is not defined` in all 3 module entries (QY15b);
  with it supplied, the next error is `customElements is not defined`.
- HN votes and collapse are GET XHRs and `fetch(...).then(r=>r.json())`
  on click, and only work when logged in (they need cookies). Note that
  "GET only" does not mean "no side effects": HN's vote is a GET.
- wire today: any method, forwards every request header from scratchy
  except Host/Content-Length/Connection, follows redirects (ureq: 5
  hops, POST->GET on 301/302/303) and does not say the final URL, no
  cookie jar, forwards `Set-Cookie`, serves `file://` for ANY readable
  file (measured: `GET file:///etc/os-release` -> 200 with the file),
  connects to 127.0.0.1/localhost on request (measured: connection
  refused = it tried), and a request to 169.254.169.254 hung for more
  than 30 s (scratchy's `fetch_raw_via_wire` has no read timeout).
  HEAD and OPTIONS work (bbc userinfo OPTIONS -> 204 with CORS headers).

[AUDIT 2026-11-16: points 5, 7 and 8 below are the pre-answer defaults.
The USER ANSWERS block further down overrides them and is what was
built: all methods with bodies (QF13), private/file targets warn and
allow, an in-memory partitioned cookie jar in wire (QF14/QF14b), and a
BLOCKING sync XHR with its own timeout (QF9). `sendBeacon` logs and does
not send, as point 8 says.]
Recommended design (fits the engine as it is: JS on the main thread,
App owns everything else, wire does the network):
1. JS side, a new `src/fetch_prelude.js`: spec-faithful data classes
   (Headers, Request, Response, Blob, File, FormData, TextEncoder/
   TextDecoder, a minimal ReadableStream), plus `fetch` and
   `XMLHttpRequest`, both thin layers over ONE native request queue.
2. Native `__net_send(...)` checks the request policy in Rust right
   away (a blocked request rejects at once with a clear log line) and
   records it in `JsRequests.net` (the QB5 request pattern). The engine
   never does I/O.
3. `App::js_sync` drains the queue and runs each request on its own
   thread (like image fetches, max 6 at a time per page). The result
   comes back as `AppEvent::JsNet(nav_generation, id, result)`.
   `JsEngine::deliver_net` resolves the promise or fires the XHR events,
   then drains promise jobs and runs `js_sync`. Stale generations are
   dropped, so a late response never reaches the next page.
4. Bodies are buffered (cap 16 MiB per response, 30 s timeout).
   `response.body` is a ReadableStream that delivers the whole body as
   one chunk. That is legal, and youtube feature-tests `getReader`
   anyway. Real streaming waits for a measured need (QF18).
5. Policy (enforced twice): scratchy refuses non-http(s) targets
   (`file:` always), non-GET/HEAD methods, mixed content and
   private/loopback/link-local hosts by name. wire refuses to CONNECT to
   a non-public address for requests marked `X-Wire-Policy:
   public-only`, at DNS resolution time, so redirects and DNS rebinding
   cannot get around it.
6. CORS like a browser for reads: same-origin is free. Cross-origin is
   readable only with a matching `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, and only
   simple requests (no preflight until QF13). `no-cors` requests are
   answered with an opaque response WITHOUT being sent (the page cannot
   read an opaque response, so it cannot tell the difference).
7. No credentials of any kind: no cookie jar, `credentials` is
   accepted and ignored, `Cookie` and other forbidden request headers
   are dropped, `Set-Cookie` is hidden from JS. Nothing is persisted.
8. XHR events and readyState follow the spec order. Sync XHR throws
   `NetworkError`. `sendBeacon` returns true and sends nothing.
   `WebSocket`, `EventSource` and `Worker` stay ABSENT, so pages take
   their own fallback path.
9. `--js-report` performs the requests too (synchronously, through
   wire), prints a `--- network ---` section, and uses the copy's
   `<base href>` as the page URL. Today it uses the file:// URL, so
   origin checks would be wrong: guardian's beacon carried
   `url=file:///tmp/...`.

USER ANSWERS 2026-11-16 (these override the defaults listed below; the
coordinator recorded them from the user's message):
- QF-D1 CORS: DEFAULT (browser rules). DECIDED.
- QF-D2a COOKIE JAR DESIGN (user, 2026-11-16, FINAL; supersedes the earlier
  per-request-name and CLI-flag drafts):
  1. The jar lives in WIRE and is ON BY DEFAULT but HEADER-GATED per request:
     scratchy sends `X-Wire-Jar: scratchy` on ALL its requests (page
     navigations, images, stylesheets, scripts, modules, iframes, fetch/XHR)
     except where the Fetch spec says no credentials (`credentials: 'omit'`, and
     cross-origin `same-origin` mode): those send no jar header and store
     nothing. A request without the header (snag, other clients) sends and
     stores nothing. Wire strips the jar headers before forwarding.
  2. ONE SHARED jar for all scratchy instances: the jar name `scratchy`. A
     `--profile NAME` command-line parameter that changes the jar name is
     LATER (not now); keep the name a single constant/config value.
  3. THIRD-PARTY RULE = "Firefox standard": Total Cookie Protection:
     cookies are PARTITIONED by top-level site. Scratchy sends
     `X-Wire-Top-Site: https://page.example` (the top-level page's site,
     scheme + registrable domain; for a navigation the destination itself);
     wire keys every cookie by (partition = top-level site, domain, path,
     name); a first-party request sees its own partition; an embedded
     third party sees only cookies it set while embedded under the SAME top
     site. SameSite is honoured as declared (Strict/Lax/None); a cookie with
     no SameSite attribute is treated as None (Firefox's current default).
     NOT done now (note as QF14b): the Disconnect tracker-list blocking of
     Firefox standard mode. [BUILT (4d62f87, bc2fb00) with two extra
     headers: `X-Wire-Nav: 1` on top-level navigations (Lax cookies sent)
     and optional `X-Wire-Initiator-Site` (Strict withheld on a cross-site
     navigation). The tracker list is QF14b2 (e).]
  4. Cookie rules otherwise per RFC 6265bis: Domain/Path matching, Secure
     (https only; loopback exempt), HttpOnly (hidden from document.cookie),
     Max-Age/Expires, session cookies, prefixes (__Secure-/__Host-), size and
     count caps (4096 bytes per cookie, 180 per domain, 3000 total: in the
     ONE limits struct). Applied on EVERY redirect hop (store each hop's
     Set-Cookie, recompute the Cookie header for the next hop).
  5. `document.cookie` get/set and later `cookieStore` use a small control
     channel to wire (a control request header/URL, e.g.
     `X-Wire-Cookie-Op: get|set` with the page URL and top site): HttpOnly is
     never returned; JS sets cannot create HttpOnly cookies. [BUILT as
     `X-Wire-Cookie-Op: get|set` + `X-Wire-Cookie-Url: <page url>` +
     `X-Wire-Top-Site` + `X-Wire-Jar: scratchy`; `set` sends the assigned
     string as the body and gets 204; `cookieStore` is QF14b2 (c).]
  6. MEMORY ONLY (QF-D7): wire keeps the jar in memory; it is lost when wire
     restarts. Persistence = a later wire flag (e.g. `--cookie-file PATH`).
     A kill switch flag `--no-cookies` is allowed.
  7. Scratchy must detect an old wire (`X-Wire-Version`, new response header
     `X-Wire-Cookies: on`) and log once that cookies are unavailable instead
     of failing. The user must rebuild/restart wire for QF14. [BUILT: wire
     answers `X-Wire-Version: 3`; scratchy logs once and sends no jar
     headers if the version is < 3 or `X-Wire-Cookies` is not `on`
     (`off` = wire runs with `--no-cookies`). Below v2 ALL page networking
     is off (QF7a).]
  8. Open and NOT assumed: an access token for the cookie control channel
     (anything that can write to wire's socket could read non-HttpOnly cookies
     through it): the user was asked and has not answered: do not implement
     a token; leave a documented hook.
- QF-D2 Cookies: an IN-MEMORY cookie jar for now (no disk). Making it
  persistent will be a separate later action item (see QF-D7). DECIDED.
  QF14 (= Q30f) is therefore READY as the in-memory jar; the third-party
  cookie rule and CORS credentials mode from the QF-D2 text below still
  need a sensible default (use: send cookies only for same-site requests
  with `credentials` 'include'/'same-origin' per the Fetch spec; third-party
  cookies are NOT sent; document it). Forms stay placeholder-only (AGENTS.md).
  [SUPERSEDED by QF-D2a above: third parties DO get cookies, but only
  from their own partition under the same top site (Firefox Total Cookie
  Protection). That is what wire builds.]
- QF-D3 Analytics beacons (`navigator.sendBeacon`, `fetch` with `keepalive`
  or `no-cors`, `<a ping>`): DECIDED (user, 2026-11-16): LOG IN DETAIL, DO NOT
  EMIT. Every beacon attempt writes one clear console line (QJ2 format) with
  the API used, method, absolute URL, content type and body size / first 200
  bytes of text bodies, the page URL, and the word `suppressed (not sent)`.
  `sendBeacon` returns true; `fetch`/XHR in this class resolve with an opaque
  or synthetic empty response, as a browser would for an unreadable answer. A
  policy flag (`beacons_send: false`) in the same limits/policy struct lets the
  user turn real sending on later.
- QF-D4 Methods: ALL methods (GET/HEAD/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE/OPTIONS) with
  request bodies, "the same way as GET through wire". DECIDED. QF13 is READY
  (wire must carry method, headers and body; QF6/QF5 codec change). CORS
  preflight (OPTIONS) follows QF-D1 browser rules. Form SUBMISSION stays a
  placeholder (AGENTS.md): this is only fetch/XHR from page scripts.
- QF-D5 / QF-D6 Targets: DECIDED (user, 2026-11-16): LOG A WARNING AND ALLOW.
  Page JS may reach loopback, localhost, private (RFC 1918), link-local, CGNAT,
  ULA and `file:` targets, and file:// pages may fetch what they ask for; each
  such request writes one clear WARNING line (QJ2 format: `scratchy: net
  warning: <page> -> <target> (<class: loopback|private|link-local|file|...>)
  allowed by policy`). Browser comparison given to the user: browsers block
  `file:` from web pages, give file pages an opaque origin, and leave
  localhost/private reachable under CORS (Chrome's Private/Local Network
  Access prompts are the tightening direction). Implementation: the policy
  struct (QF5) has `private_targets: PrivatePolicy { Block, Warn, Allow }`
  and `file_targets: PrivatePolicy`, both defaulting to `Warn`, so they can be
  tightened later without code changes. Kept regardless: http(s)/file only (no
  other schemes), the bad-ports list, mixed content (https page -> http is
  blocked except for loopback), CORS per QF-D1 for reading cross-origin
  responses, and wire's resolved-IP check (QF6) must report the class so the
  warning can be logged. Redirects re-run the policy at every hop.
- QF-D7 Persistence: NOTHING persisted yet. Mock persistence IN MEMORY (cookie
  jar, localStorage/sessionStorage, any cache) until the user decides how
  persistence works. DECIDED. QF15 is not needed now; keep every store behind
  one small interface so a persistent backend can replace it later.
- QF-D8 Sync XHR: DECIDED (user, 2026-11-16): log to the console and BLOCK
  until the request is done. `open(..., false)` + `send()` performs the real
  request synchronously on the main thread, with log lines for start, bytes,
  elapsed time and result. Risk (R8): a stalled main thread can make the
  compositor drop the client, so sync XHR gets its own SHORTER timeout
  (default 5 s, `sync_xhr_timeout_ms` in the limits struct) and, on timeout,
  throws `NetworkError` after logging. Everything is in one place (QF-D9).
- QF-D9 Limits: DECIDED (user, 2026-11-16): keep the listed numbers, held in
  ONE place so they are easy to tweak: a single `NetLimits`/policy struct in
  scratchy (QF5, src/net_policy.rs) holds every number (per-response bytes,
  in-flight bytes, parallelism, requests per page, request timeout, sync XHR
  timeout, redirect limit, URL length, beacon policy flag). No other file
  hard-codes a limit. Wire gets the per-request caps from scratchy (QF6:
  request headers such as `X-Wire-Max-Bytes` / `X-Wire-Timeout-Ms`) and keeps
  its own hard ceiling only as a backstop plus the resolved-IP public check.

USER DECISIONS (ALL ANSWERED 2026-11-16, see USER ANSWERS above; the
original option text below is kept for history):
- [x] QF-D1 (DECIDED 2026-11-16: browser rules) CORS stance. (B, default, recommended) browser rules for
      reads as above. (A) ignore CORS: every response is readable. (C)
      every cross-origin response is opaque. Security: with no cookies,
      CORS mainly protects what only the user's machine can reach
      (intranet, router admin pages, localhost dev servers). QF-D5
      already blocks those, and B also keeps pages on the code paths
      they were tested on in Chrome. Measured cost of B: none (guardian
      and bbc send ACAO, reddit's events call fails in Chrome too).
- [x] QF-D2 (DECIDED 2026-11-16: QF-D2a in-memory partitioned jar in wire, built QF14/QF14b) Cookies/credentials. (default) none, as in point 7. Or Q30f
      (an in-memory jar in wire): then every JS request also needs a
      third-party-cookie rule (does a cross-site fetch send the
      cross-site cookies?) and CORS credentials mode (ACAO `*` is
      not allowed with credentials). Without cookies these break:
      anything logged in (HN votes), youtube's EU consent (it comes
      back on every load), CSRF-token forms, reddit's tokens.
- [x] QF-D3 (DECIDED 2026-11-16: log in detail, do not send; `beacons_send`) Analytics. (default) drop `no-cors` fetches and
      `sendBeacon` (measured: guardian 9 ophan beacons in 5 s, ddg 4).
      Or send them like a browser does.
- [x] QF-D4 (DECIDED 2026-11-16: all methods, built QF13) POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE and request bodies (QF13). (default)
      GET/HEAD only. Allowing POST is what youtube's content needs.
      Without cookies a POST is anonymous, but it can still change
      server state and send data.
- [x] QF-D5 (DECIDED 2026-11-16: warn and allow, `private_targets: Warn`) SSRF policy. (default) http(s) only. No `file:`. No
      localhost, loopback, private (RFC 1918), link-local (incl. the
      169.254.169.254 cloud metadata address), CGNAT, ULA, multicast
      or "bad ports" destinations, unless the target has the same
      origin as the page itself. Blocked in scratchy by name and in wire
      by resolved address (QF6). https pages cannot fetch http.
      Alternatives: let a private page reach other private hosts; a
      user allowlist (e.g. `:set netallow host`).
- [x] QF-D6 (DECIDED 2026-11-16: warn and allow, `file_targets: Warn`) file:// pages. (default) their JS can fetch only public
      http(s), never `file:`. Today wire would hand any page any file
      (e.g. a saved HTML attachment reading ~/.ssh). Alternatives: the
      page's own directory subtree; anything.
- [x] QF-D7 (DECIDED 2026-11-16: nothing persisted, memory only) Persistence. (default) nothing on disk: no HTTP cache, no
      cookies, no storage (QY13a is the localStorage half).
- [x] QF-D8 (DECIDED 2026-11-16: log and block, `sync_xhr_timeout_ms`, built QF9) Sync XHR. (default) `send()` throws `NetworkError` with a
      log line. Alternative: block the main thread up to N s (R8: the
      compositor drops a client that stalls).
- [x] QF-D9 (DECIDED 2026-11-16: these numbers, all in `RequestPolicy`) Limits. 16 MiB per response, 64 MiB in flight per page, 6
      requests at a time, 1000 requests per page, 30 s per request.
      youtube made 337 requests in R21.

Items, in order. READY means: take it like any QUEUE item. Each lists
its prerequisites. Tests go in src/tests.rs. "Payoff" is what the R22
survey predicts; measure it with `--js-report --run-timers 5000` on the
R22 copies and paste the before/after numbers in the commit.

- [x] QF1 [S] DONE (= QY15b, commit 148b6de; implemented as pure JS in
      src/globals_prelude.js, not the encoding_rs natives sketched here, so
      no new crate was needed; the behaviour list below is what the tests
      check). `TextEncoder`/`TextDecoder`.
      Natives over `encoding_rs`: `__te_encode(str) -> Uint8Array`
      (UTF-8, lone surrogates -> U+FFFD) and `__td_decode(bytes, label,
      fatal, ignoreBOM) -> string`, or an error the JS throws as
      `TypeError`. Pass bytes as rquickjs `TypedArray<u8>`/`ArrayBuffer`.
      JS: `TextEncoder` (`encoding`='utf-8', `encode`, `encodeInto`
      returning `{read, written}`), `TextDecoder(label='utf-8', {fatal,
      ignoreBOM})` (`Encoding::for_label`, unknown label -> RangeError;
      `encoding` = the encoding_rs name lowercased, e.g. 'latin1' ->
      'windows-1252'), `decode(BufferSource, {stream})`. For UTF-8 with
      stream:true, hold back a trailing incomplete sequence for the next
      call. For other encodings ignore `stream` (note it in the commit).
      Tests: encode('€') = [226,130,172]; a round trip with an astral
      character; fatal decode of [0xFF] throws TypeError; the BOM is
      stripped unless ignoreBOM; encodeInto into a short buffer;
      'latin1' label. Payoff: github's 3 module errors `TextEncoder is
      not defined` -> next error `customElements is not defined` (QY6);
      youtube uses TextEncoder at startup.

- [x] QF6 [S] DONE (commit ce2175a; wire/src/main.rs, wire/NOTES.md). All of (a)-(f)
      below implemented, plus `X-Wire-Version: 2` on every response and

        cd ~/src/vibe-it/wire && cargo install --path . --force

      (check: a response now has `X-Wire-Version: 2`; since QF14 it is 3). Until then QF7/QF8 must
      treat an old wire (no `X-Wire-Version`) as fail-closed. DEPENDS ON THIS:

      wire/TODO.txt) Public-only connections and the final URL.
      (a) A second `ureq::Agent` built with `.resolver(PublicOnly)`.
      The resolver resolves with `ToSocketAddrs` and keeps only public
      addresses (the same list as QF5's `is_public_ip`). If none are
      left -> error "blocked: <host> resolves to a non-public address".
      It is used when the request has `X-Wire-Policy: public-only`
      (strip that header before forwarding: add it to
      `is_request_header_to_skip`). ureq resolves on every redirect hop,
      so this also covers redirects and DNS rebinding. With that
      header, `file:` gets a 403.
      (b) Add `X-Wire-Final-Url: <resp.get_url()>` to every upstream
      response and `X-Wire-Error: 1` to the synthesized 502, so scratchy
      can tell a network error from a real 502.
      Tests (existing loopback fixtures): public-only to the 127.0.0.1
      fixture -> 502 with X-Wire-Error and "blocked"; without the header
      it works as before; after a 302 fixture the final URL header is
      the target. The user must restart wire to get this. QF7 must
      fail closed: a response without `X-Wire-Final-Url` means an old
      wire, so the request fails with the log line "wire is too old for
      page networking; restart it".
      QF5 FINDINGS (wire/src/main.rs read 2026-11-16; QF6 must ALSO do):
      wire already supports every method and `Content-Length` request
      bodies (ureq `send_bytes`; an empty body uses `call()`), forwards all
      request headers except Host/Content-Length/Connection, and has no
      chunked REQUEST support. It lacks: (c) the cap headers scratchy's
      codec now sends (`RequestPolicy::wire_cap_headers`): `X-Wire-Max-Bytes`
      (stop the body at N bytes; answer 502 + `X-Wire-Error: 1` "response too
      large" instead of streaming more), `X-Wire-Timeout-Ms` (per-request
      timeout; today fixed `.timeout(20s)` in `build_agent`, shorter than
      scratchy's 30 s default and independent of sync XHR's 5 s),
      `X-Wire-Max-Redirects` (ureq `.redirects(n)`); all three plus
      `X-Wire-Policy` go into `is_request_header_to_skip` (never forwarded
      upstream). (d) DUPLICATE response headers: `stream_response` uses
      `resp.headers_names()` + `resp.header(name)`, which collapses repeated
      names (two `Set-Cookie` lines become one): iterate `resp.all(name)` and
      write every value (the in-memory jar, QF14, needs each Set-Cookie).
      (e) with the user's WARN default, scratchy sends `X-Wire-Policy:
      public-only` only when `private_targets == Block`; in Warn/Allow mode
      wire must instead REPORT the class of the address it connected to
      (e.g. `X-Wire-Target-Class: loopback|private|link-local|cgnat|ula`,
      same class names as `net_policy.rs::ip_class`) so scratchy can log the
      warning for names that resolve privately (DNS) and for redirect hops.
      (f) `file:` already answers 405 for non-GET; with `public-only` it
      must 403. The synthesized 502 body today is `wire: upstream request
      failed: <e>` (scratchy's `parse_wire_response_full` recognises it even
      from an old wire).

      Original QF7 text follows (kept for reference):
- [x] (QF7 original, kept for reference; built as QF7 da6a5c7) The engine/App plumbing.
      `JsRequests.net: Vec<NetRequest { id, method, url, headers, body
      }>` and `net_abort: Vec<u64>`. Native `__net_send(method, url,
      headers, body) -> id or error string`: it runs
      `js_request_policy(page_url(), ..)` right away; on Err the JS side
      rejects/fails and logs `scratchy: js net: blocked <url>:
      <reason>`. Native `__net_abort(id)`. `App::js_sync` drains `net`.
      Each request gets a thread running `fetch_full_via_wire` with
      `X-Wire-Policy: public-only`, 6 at a time per page (the rest
      wait in an App queue), 1000 per page then refuse, 16 MiB per
      response, 30 s timeout. The thread sends `AppEvent::JsNet(gen, id,
      Result<WireResponse, String>)`. No `--socket` -> an immediate
      error result. The handler drops stale generations. While `:help`
      parks the engine (QB6), keep results in a Vec and deliver them on
      restore. `JsEngine::deliver_net(id, result)` calls JS
      `__net_done(id, status, statusText, flatHeaders, Uint8Array body,
      finalUrl, error)`, drains jobs and then `App` runs `js_sync`. Aborted ids
      are ignored in JS. `--js-report`: after load and after each timer
      slice, drain `net` and run the requests synchronously through
      wire in order, then deliver. Print `--- network (N requests, B
      bytes) ---` with one line per request: `METHOD url -> status
      bytes`, `-> blocked: reason` or `-> error: text`. The page URL
      for the report is the copy's `<base href>` when it has one.
      Tests: a fake transport seam (a closure in place of wire) drives
      deliver_net; a stale generation is dropped; a blocked URL never
      reaches the transport; the report lists a request.
- [x] QF8 5cff36f [S] DONE `fetch()`: pure JS in src/fetch_prelude.js over `__net_request`
      (QF7). Request (QF4) validates; `data:` is decoded in JS and `blob:` read from
      the QF2 store, both without the network or the policy; http(s)/file: go to
      `__net_request` (policy, CORS, beacons, opaque no-cors, Set-Cookie hidden all
      come from QF7); other schemes -> TypeError. Bodies are buffered with the
      Request's `arrayBuffer()` (FormData = multipart from QF3). `redirect:'error'`
      rejects when the response was redirected; `redirect:'manual'` acts as 'follow'
      (no opaque-redirect, cheap enough later); `cache`, `referrer`,
      `referrerPolicy`, `integrity`, `priority` are ignored (not rejected, unlike the
      old sketch). `keepalive` is passed so QF7 classifies beacons. Left out:
      CORS preflight (QF13), cookies/credentials (QF14), XHR (QF9).

        bbc         0 -> 0   GET www.bbc.co.uk/userinfo -> 200 OK 85 B
        youtube     2 -> 2   0 req. Blockers: `MutationObserver is not defined`
                             (webcomponents-sd.js), then `TypeError: not a function`
        lobste.rs, news.ycombinator.com, wikipedia.org, en.wikipedia.org Main_Page:
                    0 -> 0   0 requests
      Live (nested dwl): a temp page showed `api.github.com/zen` (200 cors),
      a text/plain POST to httpbin.org/post (200 cors, so POST goes through the
      CURRENT wire), blob: and data: fetches; an application/json POST to httpbin is
      refused with "CORS preflight is not implemented (QF13 preflight)" (expected).
      github.com, theguardian.com (images now load with JS on) and wikipedia.org
      (language list collapsed as in a browser) look the same or better than --no-js.
      Next blockers found: MutationObserver (github, youtube; named at QY6 and in the
      older "next walls" note, no standalone item found: coordinator to file
      one); preflight for JSON POSTs (QF13).
      Original sketch follows (kept for reference):
      `data:` URLs are decoded in JS (no network). `blob:` reads the
      QF2 map. http(s): if the signal is already aborted, reject with
      `signal.reason`. Same-origin is sent as is. Cross-origin: mode
      'same-origin' -> TypeError. 'no-cors' (GET/HEAD) -> NOT sent
      (QF-D3); resolve an opaque Response (type 'opaque', status 0, no
      headers, null body). 'cors': only simple requests (GET/HEAD and
      CORS-safelisted headers), else TypeError and the log
      `needs a CORS preflight (QF13)`; send `Origin: <page origin>`.
      Readable only if ACAO is `*` or equals the page origin, else
      TypeError('Failed to fetch') and the log `CORS: no
      Access-Control-Allow-Origin for <url>`. A 'cors' response
      exposes only the safelisted headers plus
      `Access-Control-Expose-Headers`. `Set-Cookie`/`Set-Cookie2` are
      always removed. `credentials` and `keepalive` are ignored (QF-D2),
      `cache` is ignored (no cache), and `integrity` -> TypeError plus a
      log line (not implemented; never pretend it was checked).
      `redirect:'error'` rejects when the final URL differs.
      `redirect:'manual'` is treated as 'follow', with a log line.
      `response.url`/`redirected` come from `X-Wire-Final-Url`. A
      network error (X-Wire-Error, timeout, too large, unreachable)
      -> TypeError('Failed to fetch'). Abort: reject with
      `signal.reason` and call `__net_abort`. Body: QF4's buffered body;
      `response.body` is a one-chunk ReadableStream.
      Tests (fake transport): same-origin JSON round trip; a
      cross-origin read without ACAO rejects; with `*` it resolves and
      hides a non-exposed header; no-cors never reaches the transport;
      abort before and after the response; Set-Cookie hidden; data: URL.
      Payoff: guardian 9 `ActiveXObject` errors -> 0 and its geolocation
      JSON (ACAO `*`) gives the CMP its country (`CMP initialised
      without country` console.error gone); bbc `userinfo` (ACAO echo)
      resolves signed-out; ddg `/country.json` works; old.reddit's
      `fetch is not defined` is gone (its one request is unreadable,
      as in Chrome).
- [x] QF9 b676a4a [S] DONE `XMLHttpRequest` over the same queue (sync mode included; see NOTES.md "XMLHttpRequest (QF9)")
      and the same CORS code. EventTarget with UNSENT..DONE, `open(method,
      url, async=true)` (URL resolved against document.baseURI),
      `setRequestHeader` (forbidden names dropped), `timeout` (a JS
      timer -> abort the request, fire `timeout`), `withCredentials`
      (stored, ignored), `responseType` ''/'text'/'json'/'arraybuffer'/
      'blob' ('document' -> null plus a log line: not implemented),
      `overrideMimeType` (charset for text), `send(body)` (body ignored
      for GET/HEAD; other methods fail through the policy: `error`
      event plus the log line), `abort()`, `status`, `statusText`,
      `responseURL`, `response`, `responseText`, `getResponseHeader`,
      `getAllResponseHeaders` (lowercase `name: value\r\n`, sorted,
      CORS-filtered, no set-cookie), `upload` (an EventTarget that
      never fires for GET). Event order on success: loadstart;
      readystatechange(2); readystatechange(3); progress
      (lengthComputable, loaded = total = body length);
      readystatechange(4); load; loadend. On error: readystatechange(4),
      error, loadend. On abort: readystatechange(4) if sent, abort,
      loadend, then readyState 0. `open(.., false)` (sync): `send()`
      throws DOMException 'NetworkError' plus a log line (QF-D8).
      [AS BUILT, per the QF-D8 answer: sync `send()` BLOCKS up to
      `sync_xhr_timeout_ms` and throws NetworkError only on failure; non-GET
      methods work (QF-D4, QF13). See NOTES.md "XMLHttpRequest (QF9)".]

      guardian 13.

- [ ] QF13a [S] READY (same mechanism as QF7b: do them together) Redirects of a PREFLIGHTED request: today wire is told
      `X-Wire-Max-Redirects: 0` and a 3xx fails closed. Follow it by hand
      in scratchy (re-run the policy and a new preflight for the new URL,

- [x] QF14 (wire half, 4d62f87) Cookie jar in WIRE (design QF-D2a). Done in
      wire/src/cookies.rs + wire/src/main.rs: in-memory jar, RFC 6265bis,
      partitioned by `X-Wire-Top-Site`, header-gated by `X-Wire-Jar: scratchy`,
      per-hop store/send on redirects, `Set-Cookie` stripped from answers when
      the jar was used, `document.cookie` control channel
      (`X-Wire-Cookie-Op: get|set`), `X-Wire-Cookies: on|off`, `--no-cookies`,
      `X-Wire-Version: 3`. Extra headers wire accepts: `X-Wire-Nav: 1`
      (top-level navigation: Lax cookies sent) and optional
      `X-Wire-Initiator-Site` (with Nav: the site the navigation came from, so
      Strict is withheld on cross-site navigations; without it a navigation is
      same-site because scratchy sends the destination as the top site). Not
      done: the access token for the control channel (hook in
      `control_authorized`, user has not answered), the real public suffix list
      (embedded short list, see cookies.rs docs), persistence (QF15).
      THE USER MUST REBUILD AND RESTART WIRE (nobody restarts it for them):
        cd ~/src/vibe-it/wire && cargo build --release   (or cargo install --path .)
        then stop the running wire (pgrep -x wire; kill <that PID>) and start it again
        with the same command as before (e.g. `wire &`); add `--no-cookies` only
        as a kill switch. The jar is memory only: it is lost on every restart.
- [x] QF14b-scratchy (bc2fb00) [S] The scratchy half of QF14 (needs the wire above,
      version 3). (1) Send `X-Wire-Jar: scratchy` and
      `X-Wire-Top-Site: <scheme://registrable-domain of the top-level page>` on
      ALL requests through wire (navigations, images, stylesheets, scripts,
      modules, iframes, fetch/XHR) except those with no credentials per the
      Fetch spec (`credentials:'omit'`, cross-origin with mode `same-origin`):
      those send no jar header. Top-level navigations also send `X-Wire-Nav: 1`
      and (optional) `X-Wire-Initiator-Site` when a page-initiated navigation.
      (2) Page JS must not send/read cookies itself: drop `Cookie` as before and
      expect no `Set-Cookie` in answers when the jar was used. (3)
      `document.cookie` get/set through the control channel
      (`X-Wire-Cookie-Op` + `X-Wire-Cookie-Url` + `X-Wire-Top-Site` + `X-Wire-Jar`,
      body = the assigned string for `set`). (4) Detect an old wire: no
      `X-Wire-Version` >= 3 or no `X-Wire-Cookies: on` on a jar request means
      cookies are unavailable (`off` = `--no-cookies`): log ONCE, send no jar
      headers, keep working. (5) Tests with the fake executor. QF14b (separate,
      later): Disconnect tracker-list blocking of Firefox standard mode.
      DONE: see NOTES.md "Cookie client (QF14b-scratchy)". Old-wire decision:
      wire v2 strips every X-Wire-* request header; only a pre-QF6 wire would
      forward them, and the first request is the navigation (top site = its
      own destination), so scratchy sends the headers until the first response
      proves otherwise (no separate probe).

--- Tier 0: tooling that makes every later measurement honest ---------

- [x] Q1 [S] (287fdab) Make `SCRATCHY_CSS_DEBUG` tell different failures apart.
      Today `DeclParser::parse_value` clones `Declarations` before
      parsing and compares after (`css_debug_enabled`). Change it to:
      (a) report "ignored" only when no branch accepted the value, and
          stay silent when a branch parsed it successfully but produced
          the same value the rule already had. Track whether a branch
          returned `Ok` instead of relying only on `PartialEq`.
      (b) Report every selector that fails to parse, as
          `scratchy: css: dropped selector `<text>``. This is how
          `:focus` and `::before` rules disappear today.
      (c) Report every at-rule that is dropped, as
          `scratchy: css: dropped @<name>`. This happens in
          `TopLevelParser`'s `AtRuleParser` impl.
      Test: one stylesheet with a duplicate-valued declaration, a
      `p::before{}` rule and an `@supports` block. Assert on the
      captured lines. Factor the reporting so a test can collect lines
      instead of reading stderr.

--- Tier 1: whole rules or content silently lost; cheap, broad --------

- [x] Q2 [S] (66bdc60) Pseudo-classes that are dropped today (n=225 selectors on
      Wikipedia alone). Add these to `OurPseudoClass` (parse and
      `to_css`) and to `match_non_ts_pseudo_class`:
      - `:hover`, `:active`, `:focus`, `:focus-visible`,
        `:focus-within`, `:target`: never match.
      - `:checked`: matches an `input` that has the `checked` attribute,
        or an `option` that has `selected`.
      - `:disabled`: matches an `input`, `button`, `select`, `textarea`,
        `option` or `fieldset` with the `disabled` attribute.
        `:enabled`: matches any of those same elements without it.
      - `:dir(ltr)` matches everything and `:dir(rtl)` matches nothing.
        This engine is LTR-only.
      - `:lang(x)`: matches when the nearest ancestor-or-self `lang`
        attribute equals `x` or starts with `x-` (ASCII
        case-insensitive).
      Detail: "Parse `:focus`/`:hover`/`:active`/`:focus-visible`"
      (that entry's test plan applies). Done when Wikipedia's "Jump to
      content" line is gone from a real render.

- [x] Q3 [S] (5001376) Pseudo-elements. scratchy uses `NoPseudoElement`, so any
      selector containing `::before`, `::after`, `::marker`,
      `::placeholder`, `::selection` or `::-webkit-*` fails to parse.
      The whole comma-separated rule goes with it, so `a, a::after{…}`
      loses its `a` part too (n=95 selectors). Add a real
      `PseudoElement` type that parses these names. Selector matching
      for ordinary elements must then SKIP any selector that carries a
      pseudo-element. A `p::before{color:red}` rule must NOT turn the
      `p` red.
      Tests:
      - `a, a::after {color: blue}` makes `a` blue.
      - `p::before {color: red}` leaves `p` its default color.
      Generating `content:` boxes for `::before`/`::after` is NOT part
      of this item; that's Q19.

- [x] Q4 [M] (3e80aae) Per-side borders (n=150+, the biggest real visual gap
      after icons). `Declarations` and `ComputedStyle` hold one uniform
      `border_width`, `border_color` and `border_style`, and
      `border-top`, `border-right`, `border-bottom`, `border-left` and
      their `-width`/`-color`/`-style` longhands are never parsed.
      Seen: `border-top` 47, `border-bottom` 39, `border-left` 27,
      `border-right` 10, and per-side `-color`/`-width` around 55.
      - Make all three fields per-side (reuse the existing `Sides`
        type).
      - Parse the 4 side shorthands and the 12 side longhands.
      - Make `border-width`, `border-color` and `border-style` accept
        the 1-to-4-value box syntax, the same way `margin` already does.
      - Layout must read each side's width, and `paint_border` and
        `paint_border_edge` must paint each side with its own
        color/style/width. A side whose style is `none` or `hidden` has
        width 0.
      - `border-radius` keeps its current behavior when all four sides
        are identical and solid. Otherwise it falls back to square
        corners (document that as a new QUEUE line).
      Tests:
      - `border-bottom: 2px solid red` alone gives only the bottom a
        width.
      - `border-width: 1px 2px 3px 4px` sets all four sides correctly.
      - `border: 1px solid; border-top: none` removes only the top.

- [x] Q4b [S] (c13703c) Per-side borders with `border-radius`: when the four
      sides differ (width, color or style) corners fall back to square,
      and unequal sides don't get mitered corner joins; bevel styles
      (groove/ridge/inset/outset) degrade to solid in the per-side path.

- [x] Q4c [S] (96bb7b1) Uniform non-solid borders (dashed/dotted/double/bevel) with
      `border-radius` still fall back to square corners; route them
      through `paint_per_side_border` (which already handles radius and
