- [x] Q5 [S] (1000951) Anonymous flex items for bare text. Text placed directly
      inside a flex container is silently NOT RENDERED today, so content
      is lost. `build_flex_children` only collects element children.
      Wrap each run of non-whitespace text (consecutive text plus inline
      siblings, CSS Flexbox §4) in an anonymous block flex item.
      Detail: "A bare, non-whitespace text node placed directly inside a
      flex container". Test: `<div style="display:flex">hello
      <b>x</b></div>` lays out text containing "hello".

- [x] Q6 [S] (ea2eba6) `!important` (n=86 Wikipedia, 60 gentoo). Today
      `cascade_property` only sorts by specificity. Parse each
      declaration's trailing `!important`, and store important
      declarations in a second `Declarations` on `StyleRule`, and do the
      same for the inline `style` attribute (`parse_declarations` on
      `element_attr(handle, "style")`). The cascade then
      becomes: important declarations from any matching rule beat
      normal ones, and within each tier specificity decides, then
      source order. Detail: "A real CSS-gap report" (its `!important`
      paragraph). Test: `#a{color:red} p{color:blue !important}` gives
      `<p id=a>` blue.

- [x] Q7 [M] (407d3bf) CSS-wide keywords `inherit`, `initial`, `unset`, `revert`
      and `revert-layer` (n=70+: `color` 28+16, `vertical-align` 14,
      `background` 8, `font-size` 8, `line-height` 6, `display` 4).
      Nothing parses them today. Before dispatching to the per-property
      parsers, recognize these keywords in `parse_value` and record
      them per property. Semantics:
      - `unset`: same as `inherit` for an inherited property, and same
        as `initial` for a non-inherited one.
      - `revert` and `revert-layer`: treat as `unset`. There are no
        cascade layers or UA-origin split here.
      - `initial`: the property's initial value.
      - `inherit`: the parent's computed value.
      All of them must WIN the cascade at their own specificity; never
      treat them as "not declared". If doing every property is too
      invasive, scope to the properties listed above plus `font-family`,
      `font-weight`, `text-align`, `white-space`, `cursor` and
      `visibility`, and document the rest as a new QUEUE line.

- [x] Q7b [S] (c416ff6) CSS-wide keywords for the remaining properties (margin,
      padding, border-*, width/height, position, float, overflow, flex,
      grid, gap, outline, box-shadow, font-style, list-style-type,
      word-spacing) and `background: inherit` for `background-image`.
      Q7 scoped to the 12 listed properties.

- [x] Q7c [S] (042f538) CSS-wide keywords for `text-decoration`/`-line`/`-style`/
      `-color` (`inherit` copies the parent's own cascaded value;
      `initial`/`unset`/`revert` mean "not declared", ambient propagation
      unchanged), logical shorthands (`inset*`, `border-block/inline*`
      incl. -start/-end and -width/-style/-color). Not done, on purpose:
      `font-variant`/`letter-spacing`-style properties scratchy doesn't
      parse at all have no computed field to copy, so a keyword on them
      would be a no-op either way (add with the property itself).

- [x] Q8 [S] (9f77259) Charset decoding. Today `scratchy google.html` dies
      immediately with "stream did not contain valid UTF-8", because
      google.com is served as ISO-8859-1 and every input path assumes
      UTF-8: `fs::read_to_string` in the file path, the streaming
      `from_utf8()` in the stdin path, and `dispatch_wire_response`.
      - Add `encoding_rs`, a pure-Rust crate (new dependency; the user
        approved charset support 2026-11-12).
      - Pick the encoding in this order: (1) a BOM; (2) for a wire
        response, the `Content-Type` header's `charset=`; (3) a
        `<meta charset>` or `<meta http-equiv=content-type>` found
        within the first 1024 bytes; (4) UTF-8.
      - Per the WHATWG Encoding spec, the labels `iso-8859-1`/`latin1`
        decode as windows-1252.
      - Streaming stdin: buffer the first 1024 bytes to sniff, then
        decode incrementally.
      Tests: a windows-1252 byte string with `<meta charset=
      iso-8859-1>` decodes "é" correctly, and a BOM'd UTF-8 input still
      works.

- [x] Q9 [S] (f43f902) `vertical-align` beyond `sub`/`super` (n=109: `middle` 49,
      `text-bottom` 23, `top` 13, `bottom` 5, `text-top` 2). Add these
      variants to `VerticalAlign`. Two places use them:
      (a) Table cells: `top`, `middle` and `bottom` offset the cell's
          content within the row height. Check how `measure_table` sizes
          rows first.
      (b) An inline `<img>` or inline-block in `measure_mixed_inline`:
          `middle` centers it on the line, `top`/`text-top` aligns it to
          the line top, `bottom`/`text-bottom` aligns it to the line
          bottom (today's behavior).
      Text spans: treat `middle`/`top`/`bottom` as `baseline`, a
      documented approximation. Tests: one per context.
      Done. Notes: `default.css` still doesn't give `tbody`/`thead`/
      `tfoot` `vertical-align: middle` (the real UA default), so cells
      without an explicit value stay top-aligned; only `middle`/`bottom`
      cells are stretched to the row height. Length/percentage values
      are still ignored.

- [x] Q10 [S] (a4f4d91) `@supports` (n=75 blocks on Wikipedia, including the
      `display: grid` override that hides its whole left rail). In
      `TopLevelParser`'s `AtRuleParser` impl, parse the condition and,
      if it holds, recurse into the block exactly the way `@media` does.
      - Grammar: `(prop: value)`, `not`, `and`, `or`, parentheses, and
        `selector(<sel>)`.
      - A `(prop: value)` test holds if running the real
        `DeclParser::parse_value` on it into a scratch `Declarations`
        accepts it. Reuse Q1's "a branch returned Ok" signal so it never
        drifts from what the cascade actually supports.
      - `selector(...)` holds if the selector parses.
      - Anything unparseable is false.
      Detail: "`@supports` isn't parsed at all".
      Tests:
      - `@supports (display:grid){p{color:red}}` applies.
      - `@supports (display:bogus){…}` doesn't.
      - `@supports not (display:bogus){…}` applies.

- [x] Q11 [M] (33bd57f) `calc()`, `min()`, `max()` and `clamp()` (n=60+:
      `width`/`height` `calc(var(--x,1rem) + 2px)` 46, margins and
      `left` `calc(-1 * 8px)`, `max-width: min(0.85*12rem, 75vw)`).
      - Parse into a small expression tree (`+`, `-`, `*`, `/`,
        parentheses, numbers, and px/em/rem/pt/%/vw/vh units).
      - Units known when the style is resolved (px, em, rem, pt, and
        vw/vh once Q14 lands) fold to px right away. Anything containing
        `%` becomes a new `BoxLength` variant, resolved in `measure`
        against the containing block, the same way `%` already is.
      - Accept it wherever `parse_length`/`parse_length_percent` are
        used, and inside `@media` feature values, which today mark the
        query `never` (see `parse_media_query_list`).
      - Division by zero, or mixing a length with a bare number under
        `+`/`-`, makes the declaration invalid (it drops).
      Tests: `calc(100% - 20px)` against a 200px containing block gives
      180, `calc(2 * 1em)`, `min(10px, 5px)` and
      `(max-width: calc(1120px - 1px))`.
      Done. Notes: `CalcNode` tree (leaked via `Box::leak` so `Length`
      stays `Copy`) in `Length::Calc`; `%`-bearing ones become
      `BoxLength::Calc(node, font_size, root_font_size)`. vw/vh units
      are accepted since Q14 (`CalcNode::Vp`). In flex
      basis / grid track `Length::Calc` with a `%` resolves the `%`
      against the font size (wrong basis); rare, not worth an item.
      `var()` inside calc now works (Q13).

--- Tier 2: page structure and common visuals ----------------------

- [x] Q12 [L] (commit: af12757) SVG images. Today there is ZERO SVG support:
      `decode_image_bytes` only knows png/jpeg/gif/webp. That leaves
      269 `data:image/svg+xml` URIs on the Wikipedia page, and 36 inline
      `<svg>` on Wikipedia plus 34 on social.loupax.com, as blank
      space. Most icons on all these sites are SVG. Uses `resvg`/`usvg`
      (pure Rust, so the all-Rust build stays). Do the sub-items in
      order:
      - [x] Q12a1 [S] `decode_image_bytes` sniffs SVG (`looks_like_svg`)
            and rasterizes it with resvg at its intrinsic size
            (`decode_svg_bytes`); `decode_data_uri` now also handles
            percent-encoded (non-base64) payloads. Covers `<img>` and
            `background-image` data URIs. (commit: eba4122)
      - [x] Q12a2 [S] (commit: b0b4e59) Rasterize at the CSS box size when known (cache by
            `(src, w, h)`) instead of scaling the intrinsic bitmap;
            detect SVG via `Content-Type` `image/svg+xml` for wire-fetched
            images whose bytes lack an obvious prefix; 300x150 fallback
            when neither width/height nor viewBox exist.
      - [x] Q12b [M] (commit: af12757) An inline `<svg>` element becomes a replaced box,
            the same shape as `build_img_box`. Serialize the subtree
            back to XML, rasterize it, then size it the same way `<img>`
            is sized. `fill="currentColor"` must resolve to the
            element's computed `color` (substitute it before
            rasterizing).
      Test: a 10x10 red-square SVG data-URI decodes to a red pixel, and
      inline `<svg width=10 height=10>` gets a 10x10 box.

- [x] Q13 [M] (commit: b23fd53) Custom properties that cascade per element (n=300+ ignored
      declarations involve `var()`). Today only the `:root`/`html` table
      from `collect_root_custom_props` and `substitute_css_vars` exists.
      - Custom properties must inherit down the tree, so a `--x` set on
        any selector applies to that subtree.
      - `--b: var(--a)` must resolve, with cycle detection.
      - `var()` inside a quoted string must NOT be substituted.
      Detail: "CSS custom properties (v45)". Do this AFTER Q11, since
      many of these values are `calc(var(...))`.
      Tests: `.dark{--bg:black} .dark p{background-color:var(--bg)}`,
      plus a chained `--b: var(--a)`.

- [x] Q14 [M] (commit: 35c826a) Viewport units and initial-containing-block height. Add
      `vw`, `vh`, `vmin` and `vmax`; they need the viewport size
      threaded wherever `em`/`rem` are resolved. Give the document root
      a definite height equal to the window height, so
      `html,body{height:100%}` works. To do that, SEPARATE "the
      containing-block height used for %" from "total content height
      used to clamp scrolling", and don't pin scroll height to the
      window. Detail: "No viewport/initial-containing-block height
      concept exists" (it explains why a naive attempt regressed
      scrolling). Tests: `height:50vh` and `html,body{height:100%}`
      with a child `height:50%`.

- [x] Q17 [M] (dd101c7) `opacity` (n=30). The user decided 2026-11-12: multiply
      alpha down the tree. Carry the accumulated opacity down through
      box building (the same ambient path `background-color` and
      `text-decoration` use) and multiply every paint's alpha by it:
      backgrounds, borders, text, images and shadows. `opacity:0`
      still takes up layout space and receives hit-tests. Overlapping
      semi-transparent children inside one faded parent are known to
      double-blend; document that, don't fix it. Detail: "`opacity` was
      deliberately skipped".

- [x] Q34 [L] Floats across section boundaries, BFC roots beside floats,
      and `display: table-caption`. Added 2026-11-13 from a user report
      and placed ahead of Q26 because it breaks every long Wikipedia
      article (corpus page). Do the sub-items in order.

      Symptom (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nvidia, window ~1057px):
      the "History" `<h2>`'s bottom border runs straight through the
      infobox. The first thumbnail (Denny's diner) is ~800px wide, starts
      mid-page, sits on top of the infobox and hides part of it, and the
      infobox's remaining rows show out below the thumbnail.

      Relevant markup (Parsoid HTML, trimmed):
        <section id=mwAQ>                        <!-- lead section -->
          <table class="infobox">...</table>     <!-- float:right; clear:right; width:22em -->
          <p>...</p><p>...</p>
        </section>
        <section>                                <!-- sibling of the lead -->
          <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2>History</h2></div>
          <section>                              <!-- nested -->
            <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3>Founding</h3></div>
            <figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a><img width=250></a>
              <figcaption>The Denny's roadside diner ...</figcaption></figure>
      Relevant CSS (Vector 2022 / MediaWiki core):
        .mw-heading2 { overflow:hidden; border-bottom:1px solid #a2a9b1 }
        figure[typeof~='mw:File/Thumb'] { display:table; float:right;
                                          clear:right; line-height:0 }
        figure[typeof~='mw:File/Thumb'] > figcaption {
            display:table-caption; caption-side:bottom }

      Three independent root causes, all confirmed with a minimal repro
      (grim screenshot, 2026-11-13). The repro is pasted at the end of
      this item. Use it for the screenshot step.

      - [x] Q34a [M] (acbcb00) Floats never leave the `Blocks` box that placed them.
            `measure` takes `floats: &Floats` read-only. The `Blocks`
            arm clones it into `local_floats`, adds its own float
            children to the clone, and throws the clone away when it
            returns. So the infobox, placed inside the lead `<section>`,
            does not exist for the next `<section>`. Nothing after the
            lead section wraps around it, and the figure's
            `clear: right` doesn't clear it (`Floats::clear_y` sees an
            empty list). Real CSS: a float belongs to its BFC, not its
            parent. It affects every later box in that BFC, including
            boxes outside its parent, and its parent's auto height still
            ignores it. In the repro, figure B overlaps FLOAT A instead
            of sitting below it.
            Fix: let floats flow back out to the caller. Either change
            the parameter to `&mut Floats` or have `measure` return the
            grown set. The `Blocks` arm hands its updated set back to
            its caller instead of dropping it. Every place that starts a
            fresh BFC (search `Floats::default()`: float content,
            `resolve_positioned`, table cells, inline-flex,
            `measure_document`) keeps its own set and does NOT pass it
            back up. The `cursor_y`/float `top`/`bottom` values are
            already absolute document coordinates, so no translation is
            needed. Watch out for: (1) `max_bottom`'s fast-path check
            will now see floats from earlier sections, which is correct,
            but measure Wikipedia relayout time before and after (R3 in
            REGRESSIONS.md got layout down to 0.42s, so don't regress
            it). (2) The layout cache (`cache`/`ShapeCache` and any
            per-box memo). A box's layout now depends on floats from
            outside its parent, so any memo keyed only on the box +
            width is wrong when floats are present. Grep for where
            `Laid` results are reused and check. (3) The flex, grid and
            table paths must not leak their items' floats either.
            Test: `<section><div float:right w200 h300/></section>
            <section><p>long text</p><div clear:right h10/></section>`.
            Assert the `<p>`'s first line is narrower than the container
            and the cleared div's `y >= 300`.

      - [x] Q34b [M] (acbcb00) Boxes that start their own BFC must not overlap floats
            (CSS2.1 §9.5: "The border box of a table, a block-level
            replaced element, or an element in the normal flow that
            establishes a new block formatting context ... must not
            overlap the margin box of any floats in the same BFC").
            Today no in-flow box is narrowed. `overflow: hidden` gets no
            special treatment, and `display: flow-root` is parsed as
            plain `Display::Block` (see the comment at the `flow-root`
            arm of the `display` parser, which already points at this
            gap). So the `.mw-heading2` box spans the full width and its
            border runs under the float. In the repro, the red border
            crosses FLOAT A.
            Fix, in the `Blocks` arm's in-flow (non-float) branch, for a
            child that establishes a BFC (`overflow` != visible,
            `display: flow-root` (needs its own flag or variant now), and
            `display: table`/`flex`/`grid` block-level boxes):
            (1) Place it in the corridor `local_floats.avail_bounds`
                leaves: x = left bound, width = right - left. Use the
                same single probe at `cursor_y`
                that floats already use. If the box's min-content width
                doesn't fit, move it down past the next float bottom and
                try again (the simplest correct version of the §9.5
                rule; browsers do the same).
            (2) Give it a fresh `Floats::default()` for its own content.
                Its own floats don't leak out, and outer floats don't
                narrow its text twice.
            (3) Its auto height grows to include its own floats' bottom
                edges. This is the real "contain floats" behavior
                (`overflow:hidden` clearfix, `flow-root`). The comment
                near `let content_h = cursor_y - content_y;` saying a
                float never grows its parent stays true for ordinary
                boxes only.
            Depends on Q34a for the Wikipedia case: the heading and the
            infobox are in different sections.
            Test: a float:right w200 and a sibling `overflow:hidden` div.
            Assert the div's `x + w <= float.x - float margin-left`.
            Assert a `display:flow-root` div with one float child
            h100 and no other content is 100 tall.

      - [x] Q34c [S] (acbcb00) `display: table-caption` makes a shrink-to-fit table
            as wide as its caption. The `display` parser maps
            `table-caption` to `Display::Block`. The caption then counts
            as an ordinary block child of the `display: table` figure,
            so `cell_natural_width` (the float's shrink-to-fit width)
            picks up the caption's full one-line max-content width,
            ~800px on the Nvidia page instead of the image's 250px +
            borders. In the repro, figure B is 655px wide instead of
            ~122px. Real CSS (CSS2.1 §17.4, CSS Tables 3): the table
            wrapper's width comes from the table grid. A caption adds
            only its *min*-content width to that, never its max-content
            width. The caption is then laid out at the table's final
            width, above the grid or below it for
            `caption-side: bottom`. Add a `Display::TableCaption` (or a
            flag), parse `caption-side: top|bottom`, and in
            `measure_table` and the shrink-to-fit sizing
            (`cell_natural_width` on a table) leave captions out of the
            max-content width, then lay them out at the final table
            width in caption-side order. The real `<caption>` element
            should take the same path. Check that `default.css` gives it
            `display: table-caption`. Note that a non-table child of a
            `display: table` box (the figure's `<a>` holding the image)
            is wrapped in an anonymous row/cell in real CSS. Check what
            `measure_table` does with it today and make sure the image
            still sets the width.
            Test: a `display:table; float:right` box holding a 120px
            block and a `display:table-caption` with a 30-word
            sentence. Assert the box's width is ~120px, not the
            sentence's width, and that the caption wraps below the
            block.

      - [x] Q34d [S] (see PR; hash below) Investigate first: words missing from a wrapped line
            beside the infobox. In the user's screenshot, the second
            lead paragraph reads "...creative workloads, and" then an
            indented "for edge computing". The words "professional GPUs"
            are missing and the line starts ~47px in. That paragraph
            goes through the per-word path (`measure_mixed_inline`)
            because a float is active. The minimal repro at 700px did
            NOT reproduce this, so it may depend on width or on the
            infobox's exact bottom edge relative to that line.
            Re-check after Q34a/b land (they change which lines are
            narrowed). If it's still there, reproduce at ~1057px and
            find where the words go. Don't guess a fix.

            UPDATE (after Q34a-c, 2026-11-13): STILL REPRODUCES in the live
            window ("In 2025, Nvidia [became the] first company [to
            surpass] US$4 trillion": two blank runs mid-line). A headless
            layout of the saved page at 1057px (`measure_document`) places
            EVERY word, with sane x/w, and no opaque box covers the blank
            spot, so it is not a layout/wrapping bug and not an overlay.
            Next step: it is a paint-side loss. Compare the live window's
            layout width against the headless one (live text starts at
            x=34, headless at x=332, so the two layouts differ; find out
            why first), then check per-word paint (`paint` of
            `MixedPositioned::Word`, the R1 paint-cull / ink code).

            RESOLVED: root cause was cache-id instability across
            progressive layout passes, not paint. `measure_mixed_inline`
            took a fresh `next_id` for each word that fell back to
            per-word shaping (a chunk split across lines), so the number
            of ids consumed depended on line breaks, which differ between
            passes (widths are 0 until a chunk is shaped). Later ids
            shifted and read other paragraphs' cache entries (blank or
            wrong words). Fix: reserve each chunk word's fallback id up
            front (`WordSource::Chunk::word_id`). Test:
            `mixed_inline_id_consumption_does_not_depend_on_line_breaks`.
            Known remaining hazard (not seen on a page yet): `measure`
            picks the whole-buffer path or `measure_mixed_inline` by
            `content_y < floats.max_bottom()`, and placeholder heights in
            an incomplete pass can flip that, changing the ids consumed.

      Also visible in the same screenshot, NOT part of Q34 and not
      investigated: a dark square painted over "View history" in the
      page tabs, and the "78 languages" button's icons rendering as
      plain blue squares (probably `mask-image` icon cases Q29 left
      out). Look at these only if they're still there after Q34.

      Done when: the Nvidia page at ~1057px shows the History border
      ending at the infobox's left edge, the Denny's thumbnail below the
      infobox at ~252px wide with its caption wrapped under the image,
      and no float drawn over another. Take a grim screenshot of both
      the repro and the live page.

      Repro (save as repro.html, run `scratchy repro.html`):
        <html><body style="margin:10px;font-family:sans-serif;font-size:14px;width:700px">
        <section>
        <div style="float:right;clear:right;width:200px;height:300px;background:#cde;border:1px solid #888">FLOAT A (in section 1)</div>

        </section>
        <section>
        <div style="overflow:hidden;border-bottom:2px solid red;font-size:22px">BFC heading (overflow:hidden) — border should stop at float</div>
        <section>
        <p>Nested section text should wrap beside FLOAT A, which is still going.</p>
        <div style="float:right;clear:right;background:#fed;border:1px solid #888;display:table"><div style="width:120px;height:60px;background:#888"></div><div style="display:table-caption;caption-side:bottom">A long caption that should wrap at the image width rather than widen the figure to the caption's full length</div></div>
        <p>After figure B; it should sit below A (clear:right), and be 120px wide.</p>
        </section></section>
        </body></html>

--- DECISION MEMO (QY6): Web Components / YouTube and the real-site JS roadmap
--- (Opus investigator, 2026-11-16) — D0 gate CLOSED 2026-11-16 ---------

[AUDIT 2026-11-16: the D0 gate below is closed. The coordinator went
ahead with option C phases 1 and 2 on 2026-11-16 and told the user;
phase 3 (QY6a/QY6e/QY6f/QY6g) was built too. Phases 1-3 are DONE except
the leftovers still marked `[ ]` here (QY10a, QY11a, QY13b, QY15a,
QY16a, QY16d, QY18 which waits for the user, QY20b). Native Shadow DOM
moved to the QSD memo at the top.]
IMPLEMENTERS (original text, no longer applies): do not start anything in this block until the user has
ticked D0 below and written which option they chose. Until then skip
this whole block and take the next unchecked item further down.
Evidence for every claim here: REGRESSIONS.md "R21".

What was measured (build `0735ddd`): YouTube plus 9 other pages
(github, reddit, old.reddit, bbc news, duckduckgo, lobste.rs, guardian,
en.wikipedia, HN), `--js-report` and live windows with JS on and off,
and for YouTube an iterative chain of JS stand-ins until the app script
ran clean.

Key facts:
- YouTube no longer ships a custom-elements polyfill
  (`webcomponents-sd.js` = ShadyDOM + ShadyCSS only) and forces ShadyDOM
  (`ShadyDOM={force:true,noPatch:true}`), so it needs a native
  `customElements` and `template.content`, but NOT native Shadow DOM.
  No page in the survey uses declarative shadow DOM either. Native
  Shadow DOM + slots in the cascade/layout (the expensive part) has no
  measured user today.
- With 16 APIs supplied by JS shims (customElements and template.content
  with real semantics, the rest stubs), YouTube's 9.8 MB app runs to the
  end, upgrades ~100 custom elements and stamps a 1600-element app shell
  in the DOM, then starts 337 `fetch`es (1 API call, 336 icon SVGs). On
  screen nothing changes: the live window is the same grey skeleton as
  `--no-js`. No browse grid, no consent dialog in 20 s simulated.
  Next walls: fetch (Q33f, L), a real MutationObserver, quadratic DOM
  walking (QY8), then unknown (layout of the stamped DOM never reached).
- Across the other pages the most common blockers are NOT Web
  Components: ES modules (4 pages: github runs zero scripts, lobste.rs,
  guardian islands, old.reddit), `URL`/`URLSearchParams` (2 erroring,
  4 more use it), `matchMedia` (2), reflected `src` (2), `Intl` (kills
  duckduckgo's React app), `fetch` (2 erroring, everyone uses it). Visible
  CSS damage: `transform:translate` off-canvas drawers shown open
  (duckduckgo, bbc), `@layer` blocks dropped (github shows `hidden`
  banners), `<meta http-equiv=refresh>` missing (duckduckgo `--no-js`
  is blank).

Options:
A) Skip Web Components / YouTube for now. Cost 0. We lose: YouTube
   stays the grey skeleton (= what Chrome shows without JS); old.reddit's
   login form stays missing. We lose nothing else measurable today:
   github's custom elements are behind ES modules anyway.
B) Minimal stubs only (MutationObserver that never fires, AbortController,
   MessageChannel, SVGElement hierarchy, Window, HTML*Element objects,
   IntersectionObserver that says "visible", etc.). Measured: YouTube
   gets past startup ONLY if customElements and template.content are
   real; with them it builds its shell in the DOM and still shows
   nothing new. Stubs that never fire are also a trap: ShadyDOM and
   Polymer trust MutationObserver, so a silent stub turns into wrong
   rendering later instead of a clear error. Cost: about 10 S items.
   Payoff on YouTube: none visible.
C) Real implementations, ordered by measured payoff across sites
   (YouTube last). Phase 1 = cheap, correct, broad (QY8-QY14, all S).
   Phase 2 = the big enablers (QY15 ES modules M, Q33f fetch L, QY16 Intl
   M) plus four CSS fixes (QY17-QY20, S each; QY18 = translate-only
   `transform`, which the 2026-11-14 re-plan deliberately did not queue,
   so the user must OK it here). Phase 3 = Web Components for YouTube
   (QY6a MutationObserver M, QY6e customElements M, QY6f template.content
   M, QY6d re-measure), only if the user still wants YouTube after
   Phase 2. Even then YouTube content is not promised: the
   measurement stopped at fetch.

RECOMMENDATION: C, phases 1 and 2 now; phase 3 (YouTube) deferred and
re-decided after Q33f, with a fresh `--js-report` run (QY6d). Do NOT
build native Shadow DOM/slots (no measured user). [OUT OF DATE
2026-11-16: github, chromestatus, MDN and salesforce now need it; see
the "DESIGN MEMO (QSD)" block at the top of the QUEUE and R23.] Do NOT ship B's
never-firing stubs as the final state: every API added must do what the
spec says for the cases it claims, or be absent (feature detection then
takes the page's fallback path, which is usually better than a lie).
Order rationale: Phase 1 items each unblock or help 2-5 of the surveyed
pages for S effort; ES modules turn github/lobste.rs/guardian from "no
JS" into "JS runs"; fetch is what everything waits on next.

- [x] D0 (USER) CLOSED 2026-11-16: the coordinator proceeded with C
      (phases 1 and 2, then 3) and the user was told. Pick A, B or C (or a mix) and write it here. Until this
      is ticked, implementers skip this block.

Phase 1 (each S, independent; do in this order):

- [x] QY8 [S] (P2, perf, all JS pages) `nextSibling`/`previousSibling`
      in O(1). Symptom: walking a child list with `firstChild`/
      `nextSibling` is quadratic: 100 siblings 11 ms, 300 93 ms, 1000
      689 ms (R21; indexing `childNodes` is 0 ms). Cause: the `sib`
      helper in `DOM_PRELUDE` (js_dom.rs) builds `parentNode.childNodes`
      (a native `__d_children` call plus a wrapper per child) and does
      `indexOf` for every step. Fix: natives `__d_next_sibling(id)` /
      `__d_prev_sibling(id)` that look up the handle's parent and
      position in Rust (`parent` weak ref + scan, or keep the index of
      the child in the parent's `children` Vec) and return the sibling's
      id (-1 if none); same for `firstChild`/`lastChild` without
      building the whole `childNodes` array (`__d_child_at(id, i)` or
      `__d_first_child`/`__d_last_child`). Test (tests.rs, via
      `run_scripts_in` with a log collector): build 2000 spans, walk them
      with nextSibling, count == 2000, and the walk takes < 50 ms
      (assert loosely, or just assert the count and leave the timing to
      the commit message with before/after numbers).
      DONE: natives `__d_sibling`/`__d_edge_child` with a verified index
      hint (no invalidation needed); also added `nextElementSibling`/
      `previousElementSibling`. 1000 siblings 792 ms -> 1.7 ms. Commit ffd73ac.
- [x] QY9 [S] (99c1df0) (P2; ddg, reddit challenge, yt/gh/bbc/guardian use it)
      `URL` and `URLSearchParams`. Symptom: `ReferenceError: URL is not
      defined` (duckduckgo turbopack runtime, twice) and `URLSearchParams
      is not defined` (duckduckgo, reddit's challenge page). Fix: natives
      over the `url` crate (already a dependency, WHATWG parser):
      `__url_parse(input, base) -> [href, protocol, username, password,
      host, hostname, port, pathname, search, hash, origin]` or null, and
      a JS `URL` class in `PRELUDE` (js.rs) with those getters, setters
      that re-parse (`href`, `search`, `hash`, `pathname` at least),
      `searchParams` (a live `URLSearchParams` that writes back to
      `search`), `toString`/`toJSON`, `URL.canParse`; `createObjectURL`
      returns `blob:` + a counter (no Blob store). `URLSearchParams` in JS
      (string / record / sequence init, `get getAll has set append
      delete sort forEach keys values entries toString size`,
      `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` serialization with `+` for
      spaces, iterable). Test: `new URL('../a?b=1#c','https://x.org/p/q').href
      === 'https://x.org/a?b=1#c'`, `new URLSearchParams('a=1&a=2&b=%20')
      .getAll('a').length === 2`, `u.searchParams.set('z','1')` changes
      `u.search`, invalid URL throws TypeError. Re-run `--js-report` on
      duckduckgo: the two URL errors and the URLSearchParams error are
      gone (paste before/after).
- [x] QY10 [S] (P2; ddg, yt) `window.matchMedia(q)`. Symptom: duckduckgo's
      theme script `TypeError: not a function` at `window.matchMedia(
      "(prefers-color-scheme: dark)").matches`; YouTube's app script at
      top level. Fix: a native `__media_matches(query) -> bool` that
      parses with `css::parse_media_query_list` and evaluates with
      `css::media_list_matches` against the current viewport width (the
      JS env already knows it, see `innerWidth`); unknown features
      (`prefers-color-scheme: dark`, `forced-colors: active`,
      `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, `hover`, `pointer`) evaluate the
      way scratchy renders: light, none, no-preference, hover/fine
      pointer present. A JS `MediaQueryList` (EventTarget) with `media`,
      `matches` (re-evaluated on each read), `onchange`,
      `addListener/removeListener` (legacy aliases); `change` events can
      wait (note it). Test: at the default viewport `(min-width: 1px)`
      matches, `(max-width: 1px)` does not, `(prefers-color-scheme:
      dark)` does not, `print` does not; `instanceof MediaQueryList`.
      DONE: `css::media_query_string_matches(q, w, h)` (css.rs: its own
      small evaluator, since `MediaQuery` only models four features and
      treats `not` as never): `not`/`only`, `and`/`or`, level-4 range
      syntax, width/height/orientation/resolution/hover/pointer/
      prefers-*/forced-colors etc.; invalid query = `not all`. JS
      `MediaQueryList` + `matchMedia` in PRELUDE. Viewport is the fixed
      1024x768 (see QY10a). Commit 4352eb6.
- [ ] QY10a [S] (P3) matchMedia with the real window size and `change`
      events. `matchMedia` evaluates against `DEFAULT_VIEWPORT`
      (1024x768) like `innerWidth`, so `matches` never changes and
      `change` never fires. Do together with Q33b2: pass the live

- [x] QY11 [S] (ec87a8e) (P2; guardian, yt, ddg, bbc; overlaps Q33c1's "reflected

- [x] QY12 [S] (2ca83db) (P3; yt, plus `instanceof` checks everywhere) Interface
      objects. Symptoms (YouTube chain, R21 rows 3, 5, 8, 9, 11; QY4a is

      (1) `Window` with `Window.prototype` = an object whose prototype is

      that pollutes every object and breaks ShadyDOM); also

- [x] QY14 [S] (P2, diagnostics; needed to measure everything above) f50722d
      Done: (1) a throwing promise job logs `Uncaught (in promise job)
      <Name: message + stack>`; (2) the host rejection tracker feeds a JS
      queue (globals_prelude.js `__scratchy_rej_*`); once the jobs drain,
      each still-unhandled rejection fires `unhandledrejection` on window
      (cancelable PromiseRejectionEvent, `onunhandledrejection`) and, unless
      prevented, logs `Uncaught (in promise) Name: message` + stack once;
      `rejectionhandled` fires when a reported promise later gets a
      handler; (3) `scratchy --js-report --run-timers <ms>` pumps timers on
      a manual clock (simulated time, <= 5000 callbacks, per-callback CPU
      budget) and reports timer-phase errors separately (`js error
      (timers):` lines, `--- timers (...) ---` section); without the flag
      the report is unchanged except for (4): `console.error` from the page
      now has level `console.error` (stderr `scratchy: console.error:`) and
      the report counts it apart (`console.error calls: N`); engine errors
      use `__scratchy_err` (level `error`); console gained assert, trace,
      table, dir, group*, count, time* no-ops.

- [ ] QY16a [S] (follow-up of QY16, src/intl_prelude.js) What the minimal
      `Intl` deliberately does NOT do. Everything answers as en-US:
      `resolvedOptions().locale` is always 'en-US' for any well-formed tag
      (malformed tags such as 'en_US' or '' still throw RangeError);
      `supportedLocalesOf` only returns the requested `en*` tags. No other
      languages (month/day names, number separators, plural rules,
      list/relative-time words, collation order), no time zones other than
      UTC (a named zone is accepted and formatted as UTC;
      `resolvedOptions().timeZone` is 'UTC'), no calendars or numbering
      systems other than gregory/latn, no `era`/`dayPeriod` day-period
      words, no ICU-exact compact/unit/currency-name tables, `Intl.Collator`
      is a root-order approximation (punctuation < digits < letters, case-
      insensitive first, accents second), `Intl.Segmenter` is regex based
      (graphemes with combining marks/ZWJ/RI pairs, simple words and
      sentences), `Intl.DisplayNames.of` returns the code,
      `supportedValuesOf` returns [] (['UTC'] for timeZone), enumerated
      options with unknown values silently fall back to the default instead
      of throwing RangeError. 12-hour clock uses a plain space before AM/PM
      (not U+202F). Needed only if a measured page wants real localisation:
      then it is a product decision (ship ICU data or not).

- [x] QY17 [S] (DONE 3825da6: `@layer` block/nested/anonymous/statement
      order, `@import layer()`, layer-aware cascade key (u64), UA origin
      below author. Left out: `revert-layer`, `@import supports()`.) (P2, CSS; github) `@layer`. Symptom: github.com shows its
      "You signed in with another tab or window" banners although they
      carry `hidden`: Primer's `template,[hidden]{display:none!important}`
      sits inside `@layer primer-css-base{...}`, and
      `TopLevelParser::parse_prelude` (css.rs) rejects every at-rule but
      `@media`/`@supports`, so the whole layer block is dropped (18
      `@layer` rules on the page). Fix: accept `@layer <names>;`
      (statement: records layer order) and `@layer [<name>] { rules }`
      (block, nested layers become `outer.inner`); give each rule a
      layer rank; in the cascade sort, for normal declarations
      unlayered > later-declared layer > earlier layer, and for
      `!important` the reverse (CSS Cascade 5 §6.4); `@import ...
      layer(x)` (see `parse_import_prelude` in net.rs) wraps the imported
      sheet in that layer instead of dropping it. Test: `@layer a{p{color:

      green}} @layer a{p{color:red}}` is green; `@layer a{[hidden]{display:
      none!important}} .x{display:flex}` on `<p hidden class=x>` is none.

- [x] QY18 [S] (DONE 07bc68b; user OK 2026-11-16; left out: static boxes are not stacking contexts, a transformed box is not a containing block for fixed descendants) (P2, CSS; was: NEEDS THE USER'S OK: `transform` was
      deliberately not queued on 2026-11-14) Translate-only `transform`.
      Symptom: off-canvas drawers are drawn open: duckduckgo's settings
      menu (`position:fixed;right:0;transform:translate(100%)`) covers the
      right side; bbc's nav drawer (`transform:translateX(-100%);
      visibility:hidden`) leaves a scrollbar strip (see QY20). Fix: parse
      `transform` values that are a list of `translate()/translateX()/
      translateY()/translate3d(x,y,0)` (lengths and percentages of the
      box's own border-box size) and `none`; anything else (rotate,
      scale, matrix, ...) is ignored as today. Apply as a paint offset of
      the box and its descendants and the same offset in
      `hit_test_inner`; a non-`none` transform makes the box a stacking
      context and a containing block for fixed/absolute descendants (QY1
      machinery). Layout and scrollable overflow are unaffected (per
      spec, transforms do not change layout). Test: `<div
      style="position:fixed;right:0;width:100px;height:50px;
      background:red;transform:translateX(100%)">` paints nothing inside
      the viewport; `translate(10px,20px)` moves the paint and the hit
      test by (10,20).

      url=&quot;https://html.duckduckgo.com/html&quot;">` and

      The "Wikipedia" wordmark above the tagline on https://wikipedia.org
      is not drawn (two agents reported it; it was visible earlier today).
      Cause found by reading the page: the markup is
      `<span class="central-textlogo__image sprite svg-Wikipedia_wordmark">Wikipedia</span>`
      with CSS `.central-textlogo__image{color:transparent;text-indent:-10000px;overflow:hidden}`
      and the real wordmark supplied by a SPRITE BACKGROUND image
      (`.svg-Wikipedia_wordmark{background-position:0 -304px;width:176px;height:32px}`
      on `.sprite{background-image:url(<sprite>.svg)}`). Before Q33c3 (PR #121,
      transparent text honoured) the transparent text was painted opaque, so the
      plain word "Wikipedia" showed by accident. Now the text is correctly hidden and
      the sprite does not render. Diagnose which part fails (grep the portal CSS for
      `.sprite`; is the background SVG fetched through wire? decoded with
      resvg/`parse_svg`? does `background-position` with a negative y and an
      explicit width/height on an inline `span` with `display:inline-block` work?
      is `filter:url('data:image/svg+xml...')` on `.central-textlogo__image`
      interfering?), fix the smallest thing, add a test with a fixture of that
      markup. Also glance at the clipped "Search" button label on the same page
      (existing before today; file QW2 if it is a separate bug). Repro: open
      `scratchy --socket $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/wire.sock https://wikipedia.org` and
      compare with `--no-js`.
      QW1 diagnosis (2026-11-15, evidence from the live portal HTML):
      (1) `.sprite{background-image:linear-gradient(transparent,transparent),
      url(portal/wikipedia.org/assets/img/sprite-e49fbf32.svg)}` is a two-layer
      list; the `background-image` longhand only accepted a bare url()/none, so
      the whole declaration was dropped. FIXED on branch
      scratchy/qw1-wordmark-sprite (css.rs `first_url_layer`, test
      `background_image_layer_list_with_gradient_first_resolves_url_qw1`).
      (2) NOT sufficient: `resolve_background_image` (style.rs) only decodes
      `data:` URIs and nothing fetches CSS `url()` images, so the sprite
      (a 176x811 SVG made of nested <svg> sprites, positioned by
      `background-position:0 -304px`) is never loaded. This is exactly Q35a
      (external background/mask URLs). After the fix the screenshot is
      unchanged (blank above "The Free Encyclopedia"). Next: do Q35a, then
      re-check that resvg renders the nested-<svg> sprite and that
      background-position -304px crops the wordmark inside the 176x32

      are inside `@media (prefers-color-scheme:dark)` and not involved.
      Remaining shorthand gap: `background:` with a gradient layer before
      the url layer still keeps only the first layer's image.
- [x] QW2 [S] On https://wikipedia.org (both JS and --no-js) the "Search"
      button label was clipped to "Searc". ROOT CAUSE (2026-11-15): the
      portal markup is `<button class="pure-button ..."><i class="sprite
      svg-search-icon">Search</i></button>` with `.svg-search-icon{width:22px;
      height:22px}` (inline-block) and `.search-container button
      .svg-search-icon{text-indent:-9999px}` (icon-sprite hides its label by
      indenting it off-screen), `.pure-button{white-space:nowrap}`. `text-
      indent` was not implemented at all, so the label painted inside the
      22px icon box and overflowed (clipped by the button's own bounds).
      Not a sizing/italic/font bug. FIXED (ab63f1a): css.rs `text-indent`
      (<length>), inherited `text_indent` px in style.rs, paint.rs
      `paint_buffer` and `measure_mixed_inline` (the path wikipedia.org takes,
      an earlier float forces it) shift the first line by a NEGATIVE indent.

- [x] QW3 [S] (done, commit a0aa4e3; the old 79d338b was a pre-rebase hash) On https://wikipedia.org a horizontal line
      was drawn through the "Read Wikipedia in your language" text. ROOT CAUSE
      (2026-11-16, live portal HTML): the divider is a separate sibling
      `<div class="lang-list-border">` (block, position:relative, height:1px,
      margin-top:-1.6rem) AFTER `<div class="lang-list-button-wrapper"><button
      class="lang-list-button">`, and the button is `display:inline;
      position:relative;z-index:1;outline:1.6rem solid #fff;background:#f8f9fa;
      border:1px solid`. Real browsers compute `button{display:inline}` to
      inline-block, so the button is a box whose bg/outline hide the line.
      Scratchy had two bugs: (1) `<button>` stayed a plain inline box, and
      inline boxes paint only a text-run background (no border, no outline, no
      vertical padding); (2) even as inline-block, a position:relative
      `Embedded` inline item painted with its line's text instead of in the
      stacking context's positioned layer, so the later z-auto relative line
      painted over its z-index:1. Not a pseudo-element, not top:50%, not
      text-decoration; QY1/QA3/QA8/QA10 neither caused nor fixed it. FIXED:
      style.rs (button `display:inline` -> inline-block, also on the
      CSS-wide-keyword `inherit` path that re-derives display, which the real
      portal's `font:inherit`-style reset hits) and layout.rs
      `walk_negative_z` hoists relative/sticky `Embedded` inline items like
      in-flow relative block children. Test
      `qw3_divider_line_is_hidden_behind_z_indexed_inline_button`.
- [ ] QW3a [S] Plain `display:inline` elements still paint no border, outline or
      vertical padding (only a per-glyph background); real inline-box painting
      is a larger feature.
- [ ] QW2b [S] `text-indent` leftovers: positive indents (and `%`/`each-line`/
      `hanging`) are ignored; a positive/negative indent should also change
      the first line's available width for wrapping.

      positioned boxes in one stacking order per stacking context, with a
      TREE-ORDER tie-break. Symptom: www.youtube.com is a blank white
      window. Repro (headless WPT runner as in REGRESSIONS.md R12-R19, or
      any window): `<body style=margin:0><div style="position:absolute;
      top:0;left:0;right:0;min-height:100%;background:yellow"></div><div
      style="position:relative;z-index:0;background:gray;height:100px;
      margin-top:56px"></div>`: all yellow today, Chrome shows the gray
      bar. Same with `z-index:1`, and with plain `position:relative` (no
      z-index). Root cause: `paint_inner` paints in-flow children first
      and each box's `positioned` list (abs/fixed caught by that box,
      `catch_or_bubble_positioned`) LAST, via `paint_z_layer`; an abs box
      whose containing block is the root bubbles to the root, so it is
      painted after every relative box anywhere in the page, whatever their
      z-index. `is_explicit_stacking_child` only pulls out relative/sticky
      boxes WITH a z-index, and only those that are direct children of the
      box owning the `positioned` list. NOTE: QA9 as written would NOT fix
      YouTube: its tie-break puts `stacking_children` before `positioned`,
      so the z-index:0 skeleton would still lose to the z-index:auto
      (treated as 0) `ytd-app`. Fix:
      (1) give `Laid` a tree-order key (`order: u32`, a pre-order counter
          handed out in `measure`/the layout-tree build, so positioned and
          in-flow boxes share one numbering);
      (2) at each stacking-context root (the root, any positioned box with
          a non-auto z-index, `position:fixed`/`sticky` boxes; an abs or
          relative box with `z-index:auto` is NOT one, its positioned
          descendants join the enclosing root) collect every descendant
          positioned box (abs/fixed from the `positioned` lists, and
          relative/sticky with OR without z-index) up to the next
          stacking-context root, instead of only direct children; skip
          those boxes in the ordinary in-flow child loop so nothing paints
          twice;
      (3) paint negative z first (before the root's background, as today),
          then in-flow content, then z = 0/auto entries in tree order, then
          positive z ascending; ties always by `order` (stable sort by
          `(z, order)`);
      (4) mirror the same order in `hit_test_inner` (topmost = last
          painted), and keep `fixed` entries' scroll handling and `clip`
          as `paint_z_layer` does now. Fold Q55a's hoisting into the same
          walk if it is free, else leave Q55a open.
      Tests (tests.rs): the repro above with z-index 0, 1 and none (gray
      wins each time); the reverse order (relative first, abs second:
      abs wins); QA9's test (`relative;z-index:2;background:lime` vs a later
      unrelated `position:fixed` black box: lime wins); a hit-test version
      of the first repro (a click on the gray bar hits the gray div). WPT
      run (AGENTS.md step 4): stacking/z-index tests may flip, update
      `wpt-expected.txt`. Live: YouTube shows the masthead line, the
      white guide column and the grey skeleton cards (compare
      REGRESSIONS.md R20 "expected" screenshot description); re-check
      Acid2 intro, en.wikipedia Main_Page, wikipedia.org portal, HN.
- [x] QY1 done ebea7ae: `hoist_negative_z` (layout.rs) now moves EVERY positioned
      descendant (abs/fixed from `positioned` lists, relative/sticky from
      `Blocks`/`TableRow` child lists, with or without z-index) into the
      `positioned` list of the nearest stacking-context owner (root,
      fixed, positioned+z-index, sticky, opacity<1, overflow!=visible
      except `<html>`), and every such list is sorted by `stack_key` =
      (z-index, tree order). Tree order is `ComputedStyle.tree_order`, a
      counter bumped in `resolve_style`. `paint_z_layer` and
      `hit_test_inner` sort with the same key. A z-auto positioned box is
      transparent: its own positioned descendants go to the same owner.

- [x] QY5[S] (5a32839) (P3, R20 error 4) The page-load script budget is eaten by
      compiling a huge script, and the error says nothing useful.
      Error: `kevlar_base...: InternalError: interrupted` at a cheap loop
      near the top of the file. The script is 10.8 MB; QuickJS needs
      2.6 s of CPU just to compile it (8.3-8.9 s wall on this loaded
      machine), and `JsEngine::eval` arms the 5 s wall-clock
      `SCRIPT_BUDGET` BEFORE compiling, so it is interrupted almost as
      soon as it starts running (and on a busy machine any big script
      fails). Fix: (1) when an eval ends in an interrupt, log one clear
      line instead of the bare `InternalError: interrupted` stack:
      `scratchy: js: <script name> stopped: exceeded the 5 s page-load
      budget (<N> KB of source)`; (2) measure the budget in thread CPU
      time (`libc::clock_gettime(CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID)` in the
      interrupt handler; `libc` is already a transitive dependency, check
      `Cargo.lock`, else use `std::time::Instant` and say so) so machine
      load does not decide which scripts run. Do NOT raise the budget:
      the main thread is frozen for the whole run (R8 territory) and on
      YouTube nothing visible depends on this script until QY6 exists.
      Test: a script `for(;;){}` produces exactly the new message (call
      the report helper, not stderr); a cheap script still runs.

      - [ ] QY-IO-GEOMETRY [L] (2026-11-16: first slice split out as QJ4,
        then QJ5/QJ6/QJ7; QJ4 replaces the "stale cache" read below with a
        forced synchronous layout, see why there) the geometry bridge. Today JS cannot see
        layout: `getBoundingClientRect/getClientRects`, `offsetWidth/Height/
        Left/Top/Parent`, `clientWidth/Height`, `scrollWidth/Height`,
        `scrollTop` and the window's real size/scroll (Q33b2) do not exist
        or return 0, so IO/RO cannot be real. Design: after each layout
        (App's layout generation, the same place paint reads the layout
        tree) the app walks the laid-out boxes and publishes
        `Vec<(node_id, Rect)>` (border box in document coordinates, plus
        content box) plus viewport size and scroll offset into the engine
        (a shared `Rc<RefCell<GeometryCache>>` stamped with the layout
        generation; the JS thread/engine reads it, never `App`). JS getters
        read the cache (stale after DOM writes until the next layout:
        document it; a write that needs fresh geometry marks it dirty and
        returns the last known numbers). IO then recomputes intersections
        per generation and on scroll against the cached rects (threshold
        crossing, rootMargin, explicit roots with overflow clipping), RO
        compares content/border boxes per generation, both delivered as
        ordinary timer-0 callbacks after the layout event; observers with
        no targets cost nothing, and a page that never calls these never
        pays. Sizing: ~1 day for the cache + Rust publish +
        getBoundingClientRect/offsetWidth family; ~1 day for real IO/RO on
        top; hard part is the JS->App wake-up after a layout (callbacks
        run only when the event loop turns) and iframes/transforms (cut).
        NOT built here: the cache alone is small, but it needs the
        layout-generation hook in app.rs that the Q33g/Q33b2 work owns.

- [x] QA1 [S] (P1, R13) (done, commit 10e4006) `border-style` initial value is `none`, not
      `solid`. `BorderStyle::default()` is `Solid` (`#[default]` on the
      variant in css.rs), and `compute_style` in style.rs uses
      `.unwrap_or_default()` for an undeclared style, so `border: 1px blue`
      or `border-width: 4px` alone paints a solid border. Acid3's
      `* { border: 1px blue }` draws a blue box around every element (seen
      live). Fix: add a `BorderStyle::None` default (or make the cascade
      fall back to `None`), keep `border_width = 0` for `None`/`Hidden`.
      Grep `default.css` and `BorderStyle::default()` callers for anything
      that relied on "unset = solid" (e.g. `iframe`/`fieldset`/`table` UA
      borders must then name a style explicitly). Test: `div{border:4px
      blue}` has `border_width == [0;4]`; `div{border:4px solid blue}`
      still 4. Run WPT: expect new passes; a test that only passed because
      of this bug is a real regression to investigate, not to hide.
      Done: `#[default]` moved to `BorderStyle::None`. UA sheet needed no
      change (`hr`, `dialog` and the form-control rules name a style). WPT
      unchanged (249 pass). Left out / interplay: the `border` shorthand
      still does not reset an omitted style (a later `border: 1px blue`
      keeps an earlier `solid`), nor width to `medium` (Q52, still BLOCKED
      on Q52b/Q52c1; with a default of `none` Q52 now only matters once a
      style is declared). See QA1a.

- [x] QA2 (80395ee) [S] (P1, R14) U+00A0 (`&nbsp;`) is not collapsible white space.
      DONE: `is_css_ws`/`trim_css_ws` (css.rs) replace `trim`/`is_whitespace`/

      Original text:
      Rust's `str::trim()` / `char::is_whitespace` include NBSP, so a block
      whose text is only `&nbsp;` gets height 0: `has_text` in
      `collect_real_children`'s pending-run check (images.rs, the
      `!t.trim().is_empty()` test), the hang test in `measure_mixed_inline`
      (`text.trim().is_empty()`, layout.rs), the blank-word test in the
      line-box loop (`l.text().trim().is_empty()`), and `is_empty` for
      `:empty` (dom.rs). Replace each with a helper
      `is_css_collapsible_ws(c)` = `' ' | '\t' | '\n' | '\r' | '\x0c'` and
      `trim_matches(is_css_collapsible_ws)`; grep the rest of layout.rs /
      images.rs for `is_whitespace` on text content too. Acid2 loses the
      forehead and chin lines to this (both are `&nbsp;`-only lines).
      Test: `<div style="font:12px sans-serif;line-height:1em">&nbsp;</div>`
      lays out 12px tall; `<div>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</div>` is not empty.

      `<span style="display:table-cell;width:100px">&nbsp;&nbsp;...</span>`

- [x] QA3 [S] (P1, R12) (bd2945f) Margins on `position:absolute`/`fixed` boxes are
      ignored. `resolve_positioned` (layout.rs) sets `final_x` to
      `cb_x + left` or `item.static_x` and `final_y` likewise, then shifts
      the laid box there, so `margin-*` never moves an abspos box
      (`top:0;left:0;margin:36px 0 0 60px` paints at 0,0). Fix (CSS2.1
      §10.3.7 / §10.6.4, `auto` margins = 0 except the existing centring
      case): `left` set -> `cb_x + left + margin_left`; `right` only ->
      `cb_x + cb_w - right - margin_right - laid.w`; static ->
      `static_x + margin_left` (the static position is the margin edge);
      same for y with top/bottom. Subtract the horizontal margins from
      `avail` before `shrink_to_fit`. Check how `measure` already treats
      the box's own margins (it may offset `laid.x` by them), so they are
      added exactly once. Acid2: the second face line and the
      `p + table + p` scalp both depend on it (R12). Test: the repro above
      gives a box at (60,36); `right:0;margin-right:10px` ends 10px from
      the edge; `position:fixed` likewise.
- [x] QA4 [S] (065b61b) (P1, R15) The `background` shorthand must reset what it
      omits and reject junk. `parse_background_shorthand` (css.rs) skips
      unknown tokens (`Ok(_) => {}` in its loop) and the `background` arm
      in `DeclParser::parse_value` only writes `background_color` /
      `background_image` when present, so `background:red` then
      `background:none` stays red, and `background: red pink` paints pink
      (Acid2 expects both bugs absent). Fix: the shorthand sets
      `background_color` to transparent and `background_image` to none when
      omitted; accept `none` as the image; accept the attachment keywords
      `scroll|fixed|local` (see QA8) and box keywords
      `border-box|padding-box|content-box|text` (parse and ignore); any
      other token makes the whole declaration invalid; a colour is only
      allowed once and only in the last layer. This takes the `background`
      part of Q57a. Tests: `div{background:red} div{background:none}` has
      no background colour; `background: red pink` keeps the previous value.
- [x] QA5 [S] (P1, R16) (e9232f3) `data:` URLs. (a) `decode_data_uri` (images.rs)
      base64-decodes the raw payload, so the common `%2F`/`%3D`-escaped
      base64 (every image in Acid2) fails and paints nothing. Per the
      Fetch spec: percent-decode the payload first, then forgiving-base64
      (strip ASCII white space, allow missing `=` padding). (b) `<script
      src="data:…">` and `<link rel=stylesheet href="data:…">` go to wire
      (`fetch_scripts`, `fetch_stylesheets`, and the WPT renderer's
      `read_css`); wire does not decode them, and all five Acid3 data:
      scripts die with `SyntaxError: expecting ';' … :1:16`. Decode
      `data:` locally there (text: the charset parameter or UTF-8).
      Tests: the Acid2 1x1 yellow PNG with `%2F` decodes to a yellow pixel;
      `data:text/javascript;base64,%20ZD%20Qg%0D%0APS%20An%20Zm91cic%0D%0A%207%20`
      decodes to exactly `d4 = 'four';` (spaces and CR/LF are dropped
      before base64; Acid3 T97); a `data:text/css,p%7Bcolor:red%7D` link
      applies.
      Done: `parse_data_url` (images.rs, RFC 2397 + forgiving base64) and
      `decode_data_uri_text`; used by `fetch_scripts`, `fetch_stylesheets`,
      `expand_css_imports` (@import data:) and the WPT renderer's
      stylesheet loop, all before any URL resolution. Left out (filed
      QA5a): URL fragment stripping of `data:` URLs, `data:` in
      `<object data>`/`<iframe src>`/@font-face, mime sniffing.

- [x] QA9 [M] (P2, R17) DONE via QY1 (ebea7ae); SUPERSEDED by QY1 (same fix plus a tree-order
      tie-break; this item's tie-break would not fix YouTube, see R20).
      Stacking contexts compete only within one parent.
      `paint` merges `is_explicit_stacking_child` (relative/sticky with
      `z-index`) only with the same box's `positioned` list, so an
      abs/fixed box that bubbles up to the root paints above a
      `position:relative; z-index:2` box elsewhere in the tree. Acid2: the
      fixed scalp bar paints over the intro text that has `z-index:2`.
      Fix: collect z-indexed descendants (and bubbled positioned boxes)
      up to the nearest stacking-context ancestor (root, abs/fixed, or
      relative/sticky with `z-index`) and sort them together there; mirror
      in `hit_test_inner`. Overlaps Q55a (do Q55a's hoisting in the same
      walk). Test: `<div style=position:relative;z-index:2;background:lime>`
      followed by an unrelated `position:fixed` black box over it: the
      lime box wins the pixel.

below. Do them in this order. Evidence, measurements and repro recipes

- [x] QB1 [S] (P0, crash, R8) (637fef5) Never panic when the Wayland connection
      dies. Today `run()` in `lib.rs` does `event_loop.run_app(&mut
      app).unwrap()`, and winit returns `Err(EventLoopError::ExitFailure(1))`
      when `connection.flush()` fails (the compositor closed the socket).
      Fix: match the result. On `Err`, print one line, e.g. `scratchy:
      lost the connection to the Wayland compositor (it closed the socket;
      usually because the main thread was busy while input queued up)`,
      then `std::process::exit(1)`; no panic and no backtrace hint. Put the
      message in a pure `fn event_loop_exit_message(&Result<(),
      EventLoopError>) -> Option<String>` so it can be tested. Also replace
      the `.unwrap()`s on `surface.resize`, `surface.buffer_mut()` and
      `pixels.present()` in `App::redraw` with log-once-and-return, since a
      dead connection can surface there first. Test:
      `event_loop_exit_message(&Err(ExitFailure(1)))` is `Some` and
      mentions the compositor; `Ok(())` gives `None`. Live check: run in a
      nested dwl (REGRESSIONS.md recipe) and kill that dwl: one clean line
      on stderr, exit code 1, no `panicked`.
- [x] QB2 [M] (P0, R9) (d78587f) JS DOM writes must go through the restyle path.
      Symptom: a page with a 300 ms `setInterval` that writes the DOM stays
      blank forever; at 1000 ms all body text is missing and CPU is ~150%.
      Root cause: `App::js_sync` sets `layout_dirty` and bumps
      `layout_generation`, but never sets `restyle_requested`, so
      `ShapeResult` shows every incomplete pass and no complete pass ever
      lands. Fix:
      (1) In `js_sync`, set `layout_dirty`, `restyle_requested` and
          `keep_scroll_on_rebuild`, like `set_interaction_state` does. Drop
          the `layout_generation += 1` there; `redraw` bumps it when it
          actually queues the rebuild.
      (2) Coalesce: while a restyle is in flight (`hold_old_frame`), don't
          queue another one for JS writes. Keep a `js_restyle_pending` flag
          and start the next rebuild when the current one's complete
          `ShapeResult` lands. Pages with steady writes then update about
          once per full shaping pass, not never.
      (3) The very first layout of a page (no `cached` yet) still shows
          progressive passes. That's fine: the old frame belongs to a
          different document.
      Tests: build an `App` the way `r7_image_fetched_before_nav_result_
      survives_document_swap` does, give it a complete cached frame, set
      the engine dirty and call `js_sync`. Assert `restyle_requested`, then
      assert that an `incomplete` `ShapeResult` for the new generation does
      not replace `cached`. Second test: two `js_sync`s while
      `hold_old_frame` is set queue only one rebuild. Live check: the
      SoHo + `setInterval` repro in R9 shows the full article, and
      Main_Page `l h l h` never drops words.
      Landed: `js_sync` sets `layout_dirty`/`restyle_requested`/
      `keep_scroll_on_rebuild` (no generation bump). New `layout_in_flight`
      (set when `redraw` queues a pass, cleared by a complete
      `ShapeResult`) coalesces writes into `js_restyle_pending`, applied
      when the pass completes. Deviation from (3): the first layout is
      coalesced too, since the SoHo first layout outlasts a 300 ms timer.
      `ShapeResult` handling moved to `App::handle_shape_result` so it is
      testable. Test: `qb2_js_dom_writes_use_restyle_path`.
- [x] QB3 [M] (P0, R8) (dbf1610) Keep the main thread from stalling while input
      flows, or the compositor disconnects scratchy. Main_Page crashed 4/4
      with a 500 Hz mouse moving during load and hover, `--no-js`
      included. Measured stalls: hover restyle portal ~180 ms, SoHo

      (2) Hover restyles: when the last build took longer than ~50 ms,
          apply `:hover` only once the pointer has been still for
          ~150 ms (no `CursorMoved`). The pointer sends no events while it
          is still, so a long build can't overflow the socket. Today's

      (pure function of last-build time and time since last move). Live
      check: the R8 recipe (Main_Page, motion every 2 ms for 20 s) survives
      5/5 runs, JS on and off.
      DONE: (1) measured (release, nested dwl, load ~8): hover restyle

      Main_Page 341-404 ms back to back (1.03 s once); first build SoHo

The user visited https://wikipedia.org with JS on (after Q33a, `2646dd1`)
and got 12 `scratchy: js error:` lines. Investigation (fetched the portal
HTML, matched line:col in each stack against its `<script>`s):
- 2 are bugs in the Q33a runner itself: REGRESSIONS.md R5 (scripts run in
  strict mode) and R6 (error lines lose the message and script name).
- The rest are the DOM API simply not existing yet: `document.
  documentElement`/`body` are `undefined` (-> "cannot read property
  'className'/'addEventListener'/'clientWidth' of undefined"),
  `document.getElementById`/`querySelector` "not a function",
  `setTimeout` undefined / `window.setTimeout` "not a function",
  `Element is not defined` (external `index-*.js`).
These are expected for a no-DOM first slice, but they are the user's
top priority now. This block overrides everything else in the QUEUE:
do QJ1..QJ3, Q33b, Q33b1 and Q33t (Tier 7) in that order before any
other unchecked item, including Q41a+.

HAZARD, read before Q33c: the portal's CSS has `.js-enabled
.central-featured, .js-enabled .jsl10n {opacity:0}`, and the page only
reveals itself via `window.setTimeout(wmL10nVisible.makeVisible, 1000)`
(adds `jsl10n-visible` to body). Once `className=` writes reach the DOM
(Q33c) the `no-js` -> `js-enabled` swap in its first inline script
works, so WITHOUT timers the portal's main content goes invisible
forever. So Q33t (timers) must land before Q33c. Same pattern on
en.wikipedia (`client-nojs` -> `client-js` in its first inline script).

Corpus for this block (measure with QJ3 before and after each item,
paste the before/after error counts into the commit message):
  https://wikipedia.org/ (the portal),
  https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_Page,
  https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SoHo_Weekly_News.
"Done" for the block: the portal shows no R5/R6-type errors, the error
count on each corpus page drops item by item, and no page looks worse
with JS on than with `--no-js` (compare `grim` screenshots of both).

- [x] Q58 [S] (6b8f773) Multi-value box shorthands (W34): `margin`, `padding`,
      `border-width`, `border-color`, `border-style`, `border-radius`,
      `inset` take 1-4 values (top right bottom left; 2 = vertical/horizontal;
      3 = top, horizontal, bottom). Today the branches in `css.rs` parse one

      Also `margin-block/-inline` and `padding-block/-inline` two-value forms.
      Test: `margin:5px 30px 7px` gives top 5, right/left 30, bottom 7.

      already used `parse_box4`, so W34's border part was stale.
