      a fallback) — each edge's own stroke width splits into an outer
      and inner half, so a single edge shows a visible crease, not just
      a flat shade. `hr`'s `border-style: inset` in `default.css` now
      actually renders a real bevel instead of the flat solid it's had
      since before this property existed at all. 6 new permanent tests.

- [x] (96bb7b1, Q4b+Q4c) `border-radius` (v38) combined with a non-`solid` `border-style`
      (`dashed`/`dotted`/`double`, or, since v63, a bevel style —
      `groove`/`ridge`/`inset`/`outset`) falls back to the old
      square-corner rendering for both background and border — a
      rounded ring works for `solid` only (`fill_rounded_ring`). A
      correctly-rounded dashed/dotted/bevel ring needs the same
      curved-path tracing a solid ring does, just repeated per
      dash/dot/stripe/shaded-half along that curve instead of a
      straight edge — real extra work, not attempted here since nothing
      in `default.css` or a real test page needs it yet.

- [x] (dd101c7) **`opacity` was deliberately skipped, not attempted** (DONE as Q17: user chose multiply-alpha-down-the-tree; `ComputedStyle::opacity` accumulates in `resolve_style`, `paint` publishes it to `blend`; overlapping semi-transparent children double-blend, by decision) — scoping
      it out surfaced a real design decision, not just an
      implementation task. Unlike every other paint-only property
      landed this session (`outline`/`box-shadow`/v12's
      `background-color`), `opacity` can't be resolved and applied
      purely within `resolve_style`/`paint`: real CSS opacity dims an
      element's *entire subtree* as one group (so a `<div
      style="opacity: 0.5">` with text inside dims the text too, not
      just the div's own background/border), which would mean
      threading a new accumulated-opacity parameter through all of
      `build_box_for_element`/`build_table_children`/
      `build_table_row_cells`/`build_children`/
      `build_children_with_prefix`/`collect_children` — a materially
      bigger, more invasive plumbing change than anything else done
      this run (every other ambient property so far only needed
      `collect_children` itself). Worse, a simplified "multiply each
      descendant's own alpha by an ambient accumulated opacity" isn't
      actually equivalent to real CSS opacity's group-compositing
      semantics — it'd visibly diverge for overlapping semi-transparent
      content (double-blending vs. single-group-composite), a
      correctness trade-off worth the user's input rather than a
      unilateral pick between that, a non-propagating
      own-element-only version (simpler but misleading — wouldn't dim
      a `<div opacity:0.5>`'s own text/children at all, surprising for
      the property's most common real use), or the invasive
      fully-correct plumbing.

- [ ] **`letter-spacing` was attempted and reverted (no commit) after
      finding what looks like a real cosmic-text 0.19.0 bug**, not a
      scratchy bug: `Attrs::letter_spacing()` is a real, documented
      API and wiring it in was mechanically straightforward (parsing,
      cascade, inheritance, `SpanStyle`/`shape_spans` threading — all
      implemented and headlessly verified correct in isolation, single
      span, no wrapping). The problem only showed up visually, on a
      real multi-word paragraph: cosmic-text's word-wrap line-breaking
      starts firing *far* too early once `letter-spacing` crosses a
      small threshold, shattering a paragraph that should easily fit
      on one line into one-word-per-line (worse: close to one-
      character-per-line at larger spacing values). Reproduced and
      measured in isolation (not an artifact of scratchy's own layout
      code): `"Wide letter spacing "` at 900px available width wraps
      to 1 line up to ~2px of spacing, 2 lines at 3-4px, 3 lines at
      5-7px, 4 lines at 8px — clearly the line-breaking pass is
      computing available width or word width wrong once
      `letter_spacing_opt` is set, even though the *final* rendered
      glyph x-advance (confirmed separately) correctly includes the
      spacing. This is squarely inside cosmic-text's shaping/layout
      internals (`src/shape.rs`/`src/layout.rs` in the vendored
      0.19.0 source), not something fixable from scratchy's call
      site. Needs either: confirming this against a newer cosmic-text
      release (not checked — no network access this session), finding
      an upstream issue/workaround, or accepting a documented
      limitation (e.g. only support small letter-spacing values, or
      disable wrapping whenever letter-spacing is set — both would be
      lossy enough to want explicit sign-off before shipping, which is
      why this was skipped rather than guessed at).

- [x] (3c7a36a, Q25: blur, multiple (max 4), inset, currentColor all done) `box-shadow` (PROMPTS.md v27) is deliberately narrower than the
      real property: no `inset` (rejected outright, not misrendered as
      an outer shadow), no blur (`blur-radius` is parsed so it doesn't
      drop the declaration, but discarded — every shadow is a
      hard-edged solid rect), no comma-separated multiple shadows
      (just the first/only one), and `color` is required (no
      `currentColor` fallback for an omitted color). Revisit if a real
      page's shadow looks visibly wrong without blur, or needs
      `currentColor`/multiple layered shadows.

      `box-shadow: none` *is* now recognized (CSS-gap report: 10
      occurrences on the Wikipedia Main Page) — a real cascade gap, not
      a cosmetic one: a plain `Option<BoxShadow>` couldn't tell "this
      rule declared `none`" from "this rule never mentioned `box-shadow`
      at all," so an explicit `none` used to silently fail to parse and
      let a *less*-specific rule's real shadow leak through instead of
      being cleared. A new `ShadowDecl::{None, Shadow}` wrapper around
      `Declarations.box_shadow` fixes this — `none` now wins the cascade
      at its own specificity like any other explicit value. 2 new
      permanent tests (an explicit `none` overriding a rule-level
      shadow, and a real shadow still resolving normally alongside it).

      Also landed in the same pass: `text-align: start`/`end` (CSS-gap
      report: 12 occurrences) now resolve to `left`/`right` — the same
      always-left-to-right simplification `margin-inline-start`/`-end`
      already established for this engine, which has no writing-mode
      concept beyond that. (`margin-inline-start`/`-end`/`margin-inline`
      turned out to already be fully implemented, contrary to what the
      gap report's own raw count suggested — every real occurrence on
      the Wikipedia Main Page is a second, redundant declaration right
      after an equal-valued `margin-left`/`-right`/`margin` in the same
      rule, so the debug tool's own before/after comparison correctly,
      if misleadingly, reports "no change": the field was already set
      to that exact value by the physical property one declaration
      earlier. A real, if narrow, false-negative of that tool's own
      design — see its own TODO.txt entry — not a bug in this item.)

- [x] (Q13, commit: b23fd53) CSS custom properties (v45) only resolve `var()` against `:root`/
      `html`-scoped declarations — a flat, whole-document table, not
      real per-element cascade/inheritance. A `--name` declared on any
      other selector (scoping a variable to one subtree, e.g. `.dark-
      mode { --bg: ... }`) is invisible to `var()`. Doing this properly
      would mean threading a resolved-custom-props map through
      `resolve_style`'s per-element cascade plus a second, per-element
      substitution pass (an element's inherited custom props aren't
      known until its ancestors are resolved) — real, invasive plumbing
      on the order of the `opacity` item below, not attempted since
      nothing needs it yet. Also: one custom property's raw value is
      never itself substituted against another (`--b: var(--a)` inside
      a `:root` block won't resolve to `--a`'s value — only a `var()`
      *fallback* gets recursive substitution), and substitution is a
      text-level scan with no CSS string/comment awareness (`content:
      "var(--x)"` would still get substituted inside the quotes).
      Done in Q13: per-element inheritance, chained values with cycle
      detection, quoted strings skipped. Still open: comments are not
      skipped by the substituter, and `@media` conditions can't use
      `var()` (invalid in real CSS too).

- [x] `@media` (1282285, v64) now parses real `<media-query-list>`
      grammar at the stylesheet-parsing stage — `TopLevelParser`'s
      `AtRuleParser` impl recognizes `@media` by name (any other
      at-rule still falls through to the same no-op drop as before, see
      the narrower item just below) and tags every rule inside with its
      own parsed condition (`StyleRule::media: Vec<MediaQuery>`).
      Landed: `min-width`/`max-width` (the dominant real-world case by
      far — confirmed by grepping a real Wikipedia page's CSS, which
      motivated this item jumping the autonomous loop's own ordering,
      see PROMPTS.md's v64 writeup), `screen`/`all` matching and `print`
      never matching (scratchy has no print concept — correct,
      documented behavior, not a gap), an implicit-type condition
      defaulting to `all`, real comma(=OR)/`and`(=AND) media-query-list
      combination, and `prefers-color-scheme`/`prefers-reduced-motion`
      resolved to a fixed default (`light`/`no-preference` always match,
      `dark`/`reduce` never do — scratchy has no OS preference
      detection to report anything else from). `calc()` inside a media
      feature value (`(max-width:calc(1120px - 1px))`, Wikipedia's own
      literal form) safely never matches rather than crashing or
      guessing — this project has no `calc()` arithmetic evaluator
      anywhere (confirmed by grep, not built for this), and an
      individual media query that fails to parse is real spec behavior
      for "not all," not just a convenient fallback. `not` (negation)
      gets the same safe-never-matches treatment — recognized, not
      actually evaluated as a real inversion.

      Viewport-width-aware matching threads a `viewport_width: f32`
      through the one real cascade choke point (`matching_declarations`)
      and every function in `build_layout_tree`'s own call chain down to
      it, so a media-conditioned rule is only a cascade candidate when
      its condition currently holds. No new resize-reaction mechanism
      was added: `App::redraw` already rebuilds `build_layout_tree`
      fresh on every live window-width change (pre-existing, for `%`/
      `em`/`rem`/table/flex/grid measurement's own sake) — passing that
      same width through makes a rebuild media-aware for free. 9 new
      permanent tests cover min-width/max-width (including the
      inclusive boundary), `and`, comma-OR, `print`-never, the `calc()`
      case, an ordinary rule staying unaffected, a simulated resize
      picking up a newly-applicable rule, and the prefers-color-scheme
      default. Live-verified with a real `grim` screenshot against a
      real `scratchy` process (confirmed matching at this sandbox's
      actual ~1050px dwl-assigned window width); a genuinely narrow live
      window wasn't reachable in this sandbox (dwl's tile layout keeps a
      fixed-width master column regardless of client count, and no
      wlr-randr/swaymsg/wtype-equivalent exists here to force one) — the
      narrow/boundary/inverse cases are covered by the headless suite
      instead, which exercises the real matching code directly.
      Sanity-checked against real `en.wikipedia.org` (`snag | scratchy`)
      with no crash across ~100 real `@media` blocks; the page still
      renders single-column as predicted, since `grid-template-areas`/
      `grid-area` (the other half of the real fix, see `display: grid`'s
      own item above) is deliberately not part of this item.

- [ ] `@font-face`, `@keyframes`, and transitions/animations are still
      skipped at the stylesheet-parsing stage (any at-rule other than
      `media`/`supports`/`import` falls through to "invalid, drop it").
      Done and split off: `@media` (own item above), `@supports` (Q10,
      a4f4d91) and `@import` for wire-fetched stylesheets (Q18, 3a7054b).
      Still open: `@font-face` (web fonts), `@keyframes`, transitions and
      animations; none block responsive layout.

- [x] (a4b93c4, Q31: hover/active/focus/focus-visible/focus-within done, see
      NOTES.md "Interaction state"; `:target` done as Q31b (9552b38); the text
      below is history) `OurSelectorImpl`'s `NonTSPseudoClass` (now `OurPseudoClass`,
      v37) still can't parse `:hover`/`:focus`/`:active` — unlike
      `:link`/`:visited`, those need real mouse-button/keyboard-focus
      state scratchy has no concept of tracking yet (only
      `WindowEvent::CursorMoved` position exists, and only for the
      `cursor` property, not selector re-matching/repaint-on-state-
      change).

- [x] (0867a4c, Q28: `object-fit`/`object-position` and bilinear/box-averaged
      sampling done; the remainder below is history) `<img>` (v46) only decodes a `data:` URI `src` directly (base64
      form) — PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP via the `image` crate. **A real
      `http(s)://` `src` is now also handled, but only for a page
      reached by live link navigation with `--socket` configured** (v67
      — see its own `[x]` item below): `App::image_cache`, populated by
      a background `wire` fetch, `resolve_img` consulted as a fallback
      whenever `decode_data_uri` itself returns nothing. A local file
      path `src` is still *not* read at all, even though nothing stops
      it technically the way real networking does — treated the same as
      the "no networking" stance until that's revisited, not a
      near-term gap. Sizing (intrinsic from the decoded image, CSS/HTML
      `width`/`height` overriding it, aspect-ratio-preserving when only
      one axis is set) and a missing/corrupt/unsupported `src` falling
      back to rendering `alt` text are both done. Two real simplifications
      left: no `object-fit`/`object-position` (an explicit `width` *and*
      `height` always stretches independently, real CSS's default
      `object-fit: fill` — nothing needs a different fit yet), and
      `blit_image` only nearest-neighbor scales (no bilinear/smoothing —
      a heavily downscaled photo will look aliased; revisit if a real
      page's scaled image looks visibly bad).

      **Not a bug — confirmed with the user:** an `<img>` with no
      sizing CSS and no `max-width` from the page's own stylesheet
      renders at full intrinsic size and can overflow the viewport
      (worse here than on a real browser since scratchy has no
      horizontal scrolling at all — the overflow is just clipped off-
      window with no way to reach it). This is genuinely spec-accurate:
      real browsers don't implicitly cap `<img>` width either — the
      "images never overflow" behavior people expect from the web
      almost always comes from a page's *own* author CSS (`img {
      max-width: 100% }`, often via a reset), not a UA default.
      `default.css` deliberately does *not* add that rule itself — the
      user was offered it as a pragmatic (non-spec) UA-level default
      and explicitly chose to keep matching real browser behavior
      instead. Don't "fix" the overflow by adding `img { max-width:
      100% }` to `default.css` without checking this decision still
      holds.

      **Real inline-level flow, resolved (v48):** a default-`display:
      inline` `<img>` now genuinely shares a line with surrounding text
      and wraps correctly — `collect_children` pushes it straight into
      the inline run as an `InlineItem::Image` instead of always
      flushing/breaking the line (an explicit `display: block`/`table`
      override still gets its own box, same as any other block
      element). Since cosmic-text's `Buffer` has no concept of an
      embedded non-text box mid-paragraph, `measure_mixed_inline` hand-
      rolls its own greedy line-breaker whenever a run contains an
      image: splits text into words, shapes each word as its own tiny
      one-span paragraph via the existing `shape_spans`/`ShapeCache`
      machinery (full per-word fidelity — color/background/decoration/
      cursor all still work, participates in the same incremental
      shaping budget as a whole paragraph would), and positions words +
      image(s) left-to-right with wrapping. A plain-text-only run is
      entirely unaffected — same single-`Buffer`-per-paragraph fast path
      as before, only a run that actually contains an image takes this
      new, more expensive route.

      Known simplifications in `measure_mixed_inline` specifically (see
      its own doc comment for the full list): no cross-word kerning/
      ligatures (each word shaped independently); no real bidi
      reordering across words in a mixed run (cosmic-text's own bidi
      only operates within one `Buffer`, and this shapes one per word —
      RTL text spanning multiple words next to an image would appear in
      the wrong visual order); `white-space: pre`/`nowrap` aren't
      honored in a mixed run (words always split on plain spaces, no
      embedded-`\n` awareness, and always wrap at the available width —
      see the plain-text-only `white-space: nowrap` item below, which
      doesn't touch this path at all); an inline `<img>`'s vertical margin is ignored
      (horizontal margin correctly reserves line space); an image is
      bottom-aligned within its line as an approximation of real CSS
      baseline alignment (the actual font-metrics baseline offset isn't
      threaded out of cosmic-text here); only `text-align: left/right/
      center` apply to a mixed line (`justify` falls back to `left` —
      no inter-word space stretching); a line's trailing space counts
      toward its width for alignment purposes, unlike a real UA which
      hangs trailing whitespace past the edge.

- [x] (93209c2) `background-image` (v47) only decodes a `data:` URI `url(...)` —
      same policy as `<img src>` (see `decode_data_uri`'s own docs and
      the `<img>` item above) — and only the unqualified default
      rendering: tiled at the image's own intrinsic size
      (`background-repeat: repeat`, `background-position: 0 0`). No
      `background-size`/`background-position`/`background-repeat`
      longhand control at all (so no `cover`/`contain`/`no-repeat`/
      centering/offset positioning), no comma-separated multiple
      background-images. The tiled image also isn't clipped to
      `border-radius`'s rounded corners — square tile edges regardless.
      Revisit any of this once a real page's background-image needs
      `cover`/`no-repeat`/positioning to look right, rather than
      guessing at scope now.

      Q16 (93209c2) DONE: `background-position`/`-size`/`-repeat` and the
      shorthand's position/size/repeat parts now work, and the image
      is clipped to `border-radius`. Still open: comma-separated
      multiple layers (only the first layer is used), gradients,
      `background-origin`/`-clip`/`-attachment` (origin is the border
      box, not the padding box), `space`/`round` repeat (treated as
      `repeat`), `%` inside `calc()` in position/size (declaration
      ignored).

      `background-image: none` *is* now recognized — the same cascade
      gap `box-shadow: none` had (see that item): a plain
      `Option<String>` couldn't tell "this rule declared `none`" from
      "this rule never mentioned `background-image` at all," so an
      explicit `none` used to silently fail to parse and let a *less*-
      specific rule's real image leak through instead of being cleared.
      A new `BackgroundImageDecl::{None, Url}` wrapper around
      `Declarations.background_image` fixes this, mirroring
      `ShadowDecl` exactly. 2 new permanent tests (an explicit `none`
      overriding a rule-level image, and a real `url()` image still
      resolving normally alongside it).

      The `background` shorthand (CSS-gap report: 80 occurrences) *is*
      now recognized, covering only the two longhands above
      (`background-color`/`background-image`) — the dominant real-world
      case, nearly all a bare `background: <color>` or a single
      `url(...)` image. A new lenient parser, same `border`/`list-style`
      shorthand idiom (any component it doesn't recognize — repeat/
      attachment/position/box/`none` keywords, a size dimension, the
      `/` separator — is skipped rather than failing the whole value);
      `background: none` has exactly the same "leaves the property
      unset" behavior the `background-image` longhand's own `none`
      already has, immediately above — this doesn't fix that
      distinctly either. 3 new permanent tests (color-only, url-only,
      and a color alongside unrecognized repeat/position/size
      components that should still parse, not throw the whole value
      away).

- [x] `list-style: none` (fe7350c, CSS-gap report: 40 occurrences, almost always
      on a nav/menu `<ul>` that's really a link list in disguise) now
      suppresses the marker entirely — added a `None` variant to
      `ListStyleType` (previously just the 4 keywords `default.css`
      itself declares), and a lenient `list-style` shorthand parser
      (same `<type> || <position> || <image>`-any-order grammar `border`/
      `outline` already use for their own shorthands, skipping an
      unrecognized `inside`/`outside`/`url(...)` component rather than
      failing the whole value — `list-style-position`/`list-style-image`
      aren't implemented as their own longhands either, so there's
      nothing for those components to set yet). `list-style-type: none`
      longhand works the same way. 3 new permanent tests (shorthand,
      longhand, and a sanity check that an ordinary `<li>` still
      defaults to `disc`).

- [ ] No forms/interactive widgets (`input`/`button`/`select`/
      `textarea` render as plain unstyled DOM nodes, not widgets), no
      replaced/embedded elements (`svg`/`canvas`/`video`/`audio`/
      `iframe`/`object`/`embed` have no special handling or box model —
      `img` is its own, separately-tracked item, see above), and no
      JavaScript (html5ever only parses the DOM; `<script>` contents are
      read and discarded, never executed). The only interactivity
      scratchy has at all is mouse-wheel scroll and window resize — no
      keyboard input, no text selection. Fonts specifically are still
      entirely un-networked (`@font-face` itself isn't even parsed yet —
      see its own, separate `@import`/`@font-face`/`@keyframes` item
      just above). (Link navigation, and — for a
      live-navigated page specifically — fetching its own non-inlined
      images/external stylesheets through `wire`, were both carved out
      of this item and are now resolved; see their own entries below.)

- [x] **Link navigation (click an `<a href>` to load a new page)**
      (PROMPTS.md v65, 3baf01e). scratchy's own half of the two-part
      plan recorded here on 2026-10-30; `snag`'s own half (`--serve` +
      real `<base href>` output) already landed as `snag`'s own v9, in
      a separate session — see `snag/NOTES.md`/`snag/TODO.txt`.

      Landed: a real `WindowEvent::MouseInput` click handler (press/
      release close together, via a 5px `is_click` threshold, distinct
      from a drag); href recovery at exact glyph precision by reusing
      `cursor`'s own v32/v61 hit-testing (`hit_test_cursor` now returns
      `(CursorStyle, Option<&'static str>)`, href threaded as *ambient*
      propagation through `SpanStyle`/`collect_children`, same shape as
      `background-color`/`text-decoration`); href resolution against the
      page's own `<base href>` via the `url` crate's real `Url::join`
      (new dependency — `snag` already had it, scratchy didn't); the
      real `snag --serve` socket protocol (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/snag.sock`,
      falling back to `/tmp/snag-$UID.sock` — the literal same formula
      `snag`'s own `socket_path` uses) on a background thread, the same
      `thread::spawn` + `event_loop.create_proxy().send_event(..)`
      pattern `run()`'s own `Input::Stdin` case already established; a
      "Loading…" placeholder shown immediately, swapped for the real
      page (or a clear inline error — "snag server not running" for a
      refused connection, a fetch-failed message for a clean empty
      response, which is `snag`'s own documented behavior for a failed
      fetch) once the background fetch resolves; the DOM swap itself
      reuses the *same* one-shot `new_parser().read_from(...)` path
      `run()`'s own `Input::File` case already uses to build the
      *initial* document, now also triggered mid-session via a new
      `App::replace_document`. A `nav_generation` counter drops a stale
      result from a navigation the user has since clicked past instead
      of letting it clobber newer content — the one hook a future
      back/forward history stack would need, left as a comment only, not
      built (see the scope cut below).

      Real scope cuts beyond what was already pre-approved: an
      `<img>`-wrapped link (`<a href><img></a>`) isn't clickable — only
      a `SpanStyle` (text) carries `href`, never an image's own box,
      same "images don't get the per-span machinery text does" shape as
      other per-span properties here.

      **Deliberately scoped out of this first pass, per the user's own
      explicit, prior decision (not an oversight)**: multiple tabs/
      windows, still true. Back/forward history and a navigation
      keybinding — also scoped out of this first pass originally — have
      since landed (`h`/`l` bound to `:historyBack`/`:historyFront`); see
      the vim-keybindings item below.

      **Live verification, stated exactly, not overclaimed**: the real
      `snag --serve` socket round trip was verified directly against a
      real, already-running server (two real URLs, each returning
      correct bundled HTML with its own correct `<base href>`); a real
      `scratchy` process rendering a page with a real `<a href>` link
      was confirmed via `grim`; the exact bytes `snag --serve` returned
      for a second real URL were confirmed to render correctly through
      the identical DOM-build code path `AppEvent::NavResult`'s success
      branch uses, via a second independent `scratchy` process. **Not
      verified live**: triggering the click → "Loading…" → swapped-page
      transition via an actual mouse click inside one continuously
      running `scratchy` process — this sandbox still has no tool to
      synthesize a mouse click (`ydotool`/`wtype`/`dotool`/`wlrctl`/
      `ydotoold` all still absent, checked fresh this session), the same
      category of gap already flagged for `cursor` (v32/v61), `position:
      sticky` (v55), and `overflow: scroll`/`auto` (v62) wheel events.
      Worth a real interactive check next — this is the item this
      session was building toward, not another entry in that list to
      defer again.

The items below are a forward-looking plan, written up for the user to
review and prioritize before any of it is built — not yet-attempted
gaps found mid-feature like everything above. Nothing in this block has
a commit hash because nothing in it has landed. Recorded here instead
of only in conversation so the plan survives between sessions; see
PROMPTS.md's entry for the conversation that produced it for the full
reasoning trail.

Three pillars identified for scratchy's remaining scope: **styling**
(the bulk of TODO.txt above — never going to hit 100%, see its own long
tail of deliberately-scoped-out cuts), **form elements** (zero special
handling today), and **JavaScript** (none at all today). Decided: stop
trying to fully exhaust one pillar before starting the next (styling's
own tail proves that trap — every landed feature keeps growing a list
of "nothing real needs this yet" cuts); interleave based on "does a
real page need this," not "is this pillar done." JS explicitly ordered
last, per the user's own call.

- [ ] **Form elements — sequencing plan.** (SUPERSEDED 2026-11-14 by the
      QUEUE's Tier 6, Q30a-g. Focus state, keyboard input and a reusable
      `TextEditBuffer` have landed since this was written, so the
      "never had" premise below is out of date.) Two things scratchy has never
      had at all, confirmed by grep: any concept of a focused element
      (nothing in `App` or `ComputedStyle`/`Laid`), and any keyboard
      input handling whatsoever (zero `WindowEvent::KeyboardInput`/`Ime`
      usage anywhere — the `:hover`/`:focus`/`:active` comment near
      `OurPseudoClass` already flags the missing state half of this).
      Painting needs *no* new primitive — a checkbox square, button
      face, or text caret is just another `fill_rect`/`paint_border`
      call, the same primitives `paint_box_decoration`/`paint_buffer`
      already use. `default.css` currently has zero rules for `input`/
      `button`/`select`/`option`/`textarea` (confirmed by grep) — each
      falls through `build_box_for_element`/`collect_children` as a
      plain CSS-initial `display: inline` element with no visual
      distinction at all (`<input>`, being childless, renders as an
      empty zero-content inline box).

      Proposed order, by how much *new* machinery each needs rather than
      HTML spec order:

      1. `<button>` / `input[type=submit]` — click-only, no keyboard, no
         focus. Needs a real box (new `default.css` rule + a dedicated
         box-building path paralleling `build_img_box`'s own precedent —
         see the HTTP-request item just below, which needs this exact
         box first) and nothing else new.
      2. `checkbox`/`radio` — click-only toggle. One new `HashMap<id,
         bool>` (the same shape `App::scroll_offsets` already
         established for per-element live state keyed by a stable id),
         flipped on click, no keyboard.
      3. A `focused_id: Option<usize>` field on `App` — set on click
         (reusing the same stable-id scheme), cleared on click-elsewhere.
         Nothing user-visible on its own; exists purely so (4) has
         somewhere to route keys.
      4. `input type=text` (+ `email`/`password`/etc., same visual minus
         masking) and `textarea` — the one genuinely new subsystem: a
         per-element text buffer, real `WindowEvent::KeyboardInput`
         wiring (first time ever), a caret (one `fill_rect`),
         backspace/arrow-key handling. Everything else in this plan
         either reuses this or doesn't need it.
      5. `<select>`/`<option>` — last, regardless of how useful it'd be,
         because it's the one control that needs a floating open/closed
         list painted above surrounding content on click, closer in
         shape to the v56 z-index stacking-context work than to anything
         else forms-specific, not just "click" or "click + keyboard."

      `method=post` form submission needs a real request to actually go
      out somewhere — see the item just below (now scratchy's top
      priority, superseding this paragraph's original premise): `snag`
      is no longer scratchy's live-navigation transport at all, replaced
      by a new unix-socket protocol scratchy speaks directly, with
      `snag` narrowed to its one remaining job (bundling a page to a
      single file, feature-complete). GET forms, POST forms, and link
      clicks all end up going through that one same new mechanism —
      not bundled into *this* item, but no longer a separate, deferred
      cross-project change either.

- [ ] **(Was top priority; its GET/link-click half has landed — what's
      left is blocked on forms, see QUEUE at the top of this file.)
      scratchy speaks HTTP
      over a unix socket — replaces talking to `snag --serve` directly,
      unifies link-click navigation and form submission under one
      transport.** Supersedes the old link-nav-specific socket protocol
      (v65) and the narrower, forms-only "just print the request"
      sketch this item previously held — both collapse into one
      mechanism now. Decided in conversation (see PROMPTS.md's own
      entry for the full reasoning trail, not repeated here):

      - **`snag`'s scope narrows to exactly one job, and it's done
        there**: bundle a page to a single self-contained file. It is
        *no longer* scratchy's live-navigation transport at all — v65's
        `fetch_from_snag`/`fetch_from_snag_at`/`snag_socket_path`
        (talking to `snag --serve`'s own `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/snag.sock`/
        `/tmp/snag-$UID.sock` auto-discovered line protocol) are
        replaced outright, not extended or kept as a fallback.
      - **A new, separate third tool, named `wire`** owns real
        networking from now on: it accepts a request over a unix
        socket, actually executes it (TLS, redirect-following,
        decompression — reusing `ureq`, the exact crate `snag` already
        trusts for this), and returns a normalized HTTP/1.1 response. No
        bundling/inlining logic of its own — that stays `snag`'s
        exclusive job; this tool is much smaller than `snag`. Lives in
        this same repo, alongside `scratchy`/`snag`, as its own crate —
        no shared-prefix naming scheme for now (the user's own call:
        revisit a `vibe-`-prefixed naming convention across all three
        tools only if this project ever picks up a second user).

        **The daemon itself is built (`wire/`, v1 — see its own
        `PROMPTS.md`/`NOTES.md`/`TODO.txt`)**: request parsing
        (absolute-form request line, headers, `Content-Length` body),
        real execution via `ureq` (TLS/redirects/gzip decompression),
        response normalization (a real 4xx/5xx from upstream forwards
        as itself, not a transport error; `Transfer-Encoding`/
        `Content-Encoding` are stripped and a fresh `Content-Length`
        is computed over the already-decoded body; a genuine transport
        failure — DNS/connect/TLS — synthesizes a `502` rather than
        hanging or crashing the connection), and the daemon's own
        `--socket`-overridable default-path/stale-socket-cleanup/
        concurrent-connections-via-one-thread-per-connection plumbing,
        all covered by 10 permanent tests (including one real Unix-
        socket round trip).

        **`scratchy`'s own side — the GET/link-click half — is also
        built now (v66)**: `fetch_from_snag`/`fetch_from_snag_at`/
        `snag_socket_path` are gone, replaced by `build_get_request_
        text`/`fetch_via_wire_at`/`parse_wire_response`/`dispatch_wire_
        response` (a real absolute-form request, real response-framing
        dispatch by `Content-Type`, a fetched image wrapped into a
        synthetic `<img src="data:...">` document rather than a second
        rendering mode), a new strict-opt-in `--socket <path>` CLI flag
        (no auto-discovered default), and the stdout-request-print
        fallback for "no socket configured or configured one
        unreachable." 8 new permanent tests. Live-verified further than
        any prior navigation work in this project: a real synthesized
        `xdotool` click, inside a real running `scratchy` process,
        against a real on-screen link, triggered real navigation through
        a real running `wire` daemon to a real local HTTP fixture — for
        both a fetched HTML page and a fetched image — confirmed via
        before/after screenshots (see `PROMPTS.md`'s own v66 entry).

        **A live-navigated page's own non-inlined sub-resources are
        also fetched now (v67)** — the gap `snag`'s own inlining always
        covered for a bundled page, but nothing did for one reached by
        clicking a link, since that path never goes through `snag` at
        all. `process_external_resources` (`start_navigation`'s own
        background thread, after the top-level page itself comes back)
        parses the fetched HTML into a throwaway DOM just long enough to
        find every `<link rel=stylesheet href>`/non-`data:` `<img src>`,
        resolved against the page's own `<base href>` (or its URL, if it
        has none). Stylesheets are fetched over `wire` *synchronously*,
        right there, and concatenated into `App::extra_css` — present
        for the page's very first layout, no flash of unstyled content.
        Images are fetched asynchronously instead — each its own
        `thread::spawn`, reported back via a new `AppEvent::
        ImageFetched` and cached in `App::image_cache` (keyed by the
        raw, unresolved `src` text — safe only because `replace_
        document` clears it on every navigation) — so the page renders
        immediately and each image materializes as its own fetch lands,
        never blocking the rest of the page. `resolve_img` falls back to
        this cache for any `src` `decode_data_uri` doesn't recognize. 9
        new permanent tests (177 total, up from 168 — fetch/content-
        type-dispatch/DOM-scan/cache-lookup/cascade-merge functions each
        covered individually; the thin orchestration
        between them rests on code review + live verification, same
        category as `start_navigation`'s own click wiring already is).
        Live-verified past even v66's own bar: a real click through a
        real `wire` daemon to a real local HTTP server picked up a real
        external stylesheet (applied before first paint) and a real
        fetched image; a deliberately slow image route confirmed the
        page rendered and was usable *before* the image arrived, then
        the image appeared on its own with no further click — see
        `PROMPTS.md`'s own v67 entry for the full screenshot trail.

        **Still fully open**: `<button>`/form-element support doesn't
        exist at all yet (no box, no focus tracking, no keyboard
        handling — see the sequencing-plan item above), so form
        submission can't be wired to `wire` until that lands first;
        `snag` itself hasn't been narrowed yet (`--serve` is still
        there, unremoved, even though nothing calls it anymore —
        removing it is `snag`'s own change to make, not scratchy's, and
        is safe to do independently whenever `snag`'s own session picks
        it up); and sub-resource fetching only happens for a *live-
        navigated* page — `scratchy --socket <path> some-static-file.html`
        and `snag url | scratchy --socket <path>` never call `process_
        external_resources` at all, even with a socket configured, so a
        static/streamed page's own un-inlined references (if any — a
        `snag`-bundled one never has any to begin with) still won't
        resolve. Not attempted: nothing has needed it, and `App::new_
        static`/`new_streaming` would need their own trigger point for
        it, a genuinely separate piece of plumbing from `start_
        navigation`'s.
      - **Request framing**: a real HTTP/1.1 request, using the
        standard proxy "absolute-form" request line (`GET https://
        example.com/path HTTP/1.1`, RFC 7230 §5.3.2) rather than the
        origin-relative form a direct browser connection would send —
        this is what lets a daemon behind a bare unix socket (no DNS/
        TLS-SNI of its own to infer a destination from) know exactly
        where to actually connect, using only standard HTTP syntax, no
        invented command/framing layer on top.
      - **Response framing — the one point actually debated and
        resolved in conversation**: the daemon does *not* relay raw
        bytes 1:1 (that would leave chunked-decoding/redirect-looping/
        gzip-decoding as new HTTP-client logic inside scratchy, which
        contradicts "scratchy still doesn't do its own networking").
        It also doesn't try to collapse different content kinds into
        one shape — a fetched `index.html` and a fetched `photo.jpg`
        are genuinely different things scratchy must handle
        differently. The resolved contract: the daemon returns a clean,
        already-decoded HTTP/1.1 response — real headers, redirects
        already followed, body already decompressed, `Content-Length`
        never `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` — and scratchy reads exactly
        one header out of it to decide what the body *is*:
        `Content-Type`. `text/html` → the existing HTML-parse path
        (`new_parser().read_from(...)`, same as v65's `AppEvent::
        NavResult` success branch already uses). `image/png`/`jpeg`/
        `gif`/`webp` → the existing `decode_image_bytes` path (already
        built and tested for `data:` URI images — reusable verbatim on
        real fetched bytes, no new image-decoding code). Anything else,
        for now: a clear "can't render this content type" inline
        message, never an attempt to force unknown bytes through either
        path. Every other header (`Cache-Control`, `Server`, cookies,
        etc.) is read by nobody and simply discarded — confirmed with
        the user directly: normalizing the *transport* mess is the
        daemon's job, but deciding what the bytes mean stays scratchy's.
      - **One connection per request on the scratchy↔daemon leg, no
        multiplexing there — but concurrent *separate* connections are
        expected, not excluded.** Same dead-simple shape `snag --serve`'s
        own protocol already has: connect, write the request, read
        until the daemon closes, done, no request IDs or shared-
        connection framing. Discussed and settled in conversation: real
        multiplexing (interleaving several requests' bytes over one
        shared connection) would need both sides to agree on framing —
        it can't be hidden in the daemon alone, since scratchy is the
        other half of that exchange and would need new ID-tagging/demux
        code regardless of how smart the daemon is — and it would buy
        nothing here anyway, since a unix socket is local IPC with no
        handshake; opening several concurrent one-shot connections
        (future per-resource fetches firing off N background threads,
        the same shape `start_navigation`'s background thread already
        uses, just invoked more than once at a time) costs about as much
        as opening one. The real place for connection pooling/reuse is
        the *daemon's own upstream leg*, talking to real remote servers
        over actual TCP/TLS — the daemon is free to reuse/pool its own
        connections to the same remote host across multiple client
        requests however it likes, entirely invisible to scratchy, with
        zero protocol impact on the scratchy-facing socket. That's where
        the real performance cost (handshakes, round trips to a real
        server) actually lives, and it's already implied by "the daemon
        owns networking" — nothing new to design there, just a reminder
        not to conflate the two legs.
      - **`--socket <path>` becomes an explicit opt-in CLI flag**,
        replacing today's fixed-path auto-discovery entirely. No flag
        given, or any connection failure against the given path: falls
        all the way through to printing the raw request text to stdout
        (the literal HTTP/1.1 wire text — request line, `Host`, other
        headers, blank line, body — chosen specifically so it's directly
        replayable by `nc`/a raw socket/this same new daemon run
        manually, with zero translation) plus the existing inline
        "not available" status-message precedent (v65's connection-
        refused handling) — scratchy never gets a response back in this
        branch, by design; that's the whole point of the fallback.
      - **Forms and link-clicks become the exact same code path**: both
        reduce to "build a request (GET for a link or a `method=get`
        form, GET/POST for a form depending on its own `method`
        attribute), try the socket, else print" — resolving the
        asymmetry this conversation flagged earlier (link-nav always
        navigated live, forms always only printed; now both depend
        purely on whether `--socket` is active and reachable, not on
        which kind of control was clicked). Form value-collection itself
        (walk to the nearest ancestor `form` via the existing
        `parent_handle`/`El` machinery; read `value`/
        `checked`/selected-`<option>`/textarea-text from the live DOM
        per control type; encode via `url::form_urlencoded`, already a
        dependency; resolve `action` against `base_url` via the existing
        `resolve_href`) is unchanged from this item's
        earlier draft — only the transport underneath it changed.
      - **Click-side rewrite this item actually requires**:
        `build_box_for_element` needs the same early-
        return shape it already gives `img` (via
        `build_img_box`) for `<button>`/`input[type=submit]`, with
        a new `submit_handle: Option<Handle>` field on `LayoutBox`
        (`None` at its ~9 other construction sites) so
        `hit_test_cursor` (already a 2-tuple with `href` —
        widens to a 3-tuple, same shape v65's own `href` addition used)
        can report back *which* element was clicked. `start_navigation`
        and the `WindowEvent::MouseInput` handler both get rebuilt around the new socket protocol
        and request-building rather than the old bare-URL-line one.
      - **Still just a transport, not a solution to async resource
        loading**: this unblocks future `<img src="https://…">` fetches
        (same daemon, same request/response shape, same Content-Type
        dispatch) but doesn't by itself implement them — that still
        needs the separate, not-yet-built per-resource cache/placeholder/
        redraw mechanism this conversation discussed (a `HashMap<url,
        Rc<DecodedImage>>` on `App`, same shape `scroll_offsets`
        established, populated by a background fetch, same "fire a
        background thread + send an `AppEvent` back into the winit
        loop" shape `start_navigation` already uses for whole-document
        navigation).
      - The request-building and response-dispatch logic is pure-
        function testable with no `App`/winit involved (parse a fixture
        document/response, call the functions directly, assert on exact
        bytes) — same "isolate what can be isolated" precedent v65's own
        testing-convention note already established. Only the actual
        socket I/O and `WindowEvent::MouseInput` wiring land in the
        unverified-live-in-this-sandbox bucket v32/v55/v62/v65 already
        flagged (no synthetic mouse/keyboard event tool here).

- [x] (Q1 follow-up done, see QUEUE; dropped selectors/at-rules now reported, duplicate-valued declarations silent.)
- [x] **Debug tools: `SCRATCHY_CSS_DEBUG=1` prints every CSS declaration
      scratchy's cascade couldn't act on.** The user's own request,
      picked up once the real motivating pain (diagnosing the `grid-
      template-areas`/`minmax()` gap by manually `curl`-ing Wikipedia's
      CSS and grepping it by hand) was already fresh. Resolves this
      item's own open questions from when it was first recorded:

      - **Env var, not a CLI flag** — `SCRATCHY_CSS_DEBUG`, the same
        env-var-gated, zero-cost-when-off shape `SCRATCHY_TIMING`/
        `snag`'s own `SNAG_TIMING`/`wire`'s own `WIRE_TIMING` already
        established (`css_debug_enabled`), not a new convention invented
        for this one feature.
      - **Hooked with zero changes to any of the ~85 per-property
        `if let Ok(v) = parse_x(input) { ... }` branches in `DeclParser::
        parse_value`**, avoiding exactly the invasive rewrite this item
        originally worried about: `Declarations` now derives `PartialEq`
        (and the 9 field types that didn't already have it — `Length`,
        `MaxLen`, `FontWeightValue`, `DecorationValue`, `LineHeight`,
        `BoxShadow`, `FlexBasis`, `GridTrackSpec`, `TrackBreadth` — do
        now too), so `parse_value` just clones `self.decls` before
        running its own unchanged dispatch chain and compares after —
        unchanged means this declaration had no effect, whether `name`
        matched no branch at all or matched one whose own value shape
        wasn't supported, without needing to know which case it was.
      - **Reports at the raw-declaration level** (`property: value`,
        exactly as the stylesheet wrote it), not resolved to "which
        `TODO.txt` item this maps to" — the cheaper, honest answer this
        item's own open question favored; the raw value text is
        captured the same way the `--name` custom-property branch
        already captures its own (consume to the end, slice, reset).
      - **Not deduplicated** — a thousand elements declaring the same
        unsupported value print a thousand identical lines; summarizing
        would need to buffer state across the whole parse, real but
        separate scope past "see that something's being dropped at
        all."

      2 new permanent tests: one confirming `Declarations`'s own
      `PartialEq` genuinely distinguishes "something changed" from
      "nothing changed," one exercising the real end-to-end premise
      through `parse_stylesheet` itself (a known-unsupported `grid-area`
      numeric form leaves its field unset while a supported sibling
      declaration in the same rule still applies).

      **Not live-verified this session, flagged rather than glossed
      over, same category as v32/v55/v62/v65's own gaps**: this
      sandbox's actual display was asleep (no DPMS/compositor frame
      callbacks arriving) for the entire window this was built in —
      confirmed, not assumed, by tracing `window_event`/`redraw` directly
      and finding the real stall: `redraw`'s own two-tick "wait for the
      tiling WM to settle" optimization (added for performance, see
      `PROGRESSIVE_LAYOUT_TICK_BUDGET`'s neighborhood) never gets its
      second, compositor-paced frame, so the very first CSS parse never
      runs at all — not a bug in this feature, confirmed by the same
      stall blocking `resumed()`'s already-landed window entirely, with
      or without `SCRATCHY_CSS_DEBUG` involved. Worth a real `SCRATCHY_
      CSS_DEBUG=1 scratchy <page-with-known-gaps>` check next time a
      human's at the keyboard with the screen actually on.

- [x] **A real CSS-gap report, built from `SCRATCHY_CSS_DEBUG=1` against
      a real page (the Wikipedia Main Page), used to prioritize what's
      below** — and two real corrections the report itself needed before
      anything got built off of it, worth recording precisely rather than
      quietly fixed: an `!important` suffix does **not** actually block
      value parsing today (verified directly: `color: red !important`
      parses and applies correctly, since `CssColor::parse` stops cleanly
      before the trailing `!important` tokens and `parse_value` already
      tolerates whatever's left over) — every sample the report first
      attributed to `!important` was independently broken by an unrelated,
      already-known gap (an unsupported keyword, a missing shorthand, a
      missing property), confirmed by re-checking each one with the
      `!important` suffix removed. What *is* still real and still
      unimplemented: the cascade (`cascade_property`) has no concept of
      importance at all — purely resolves by specificity — so an
      `!important` declaration still can't out-rank a more specific
      competing rule; a real, narrow correctness gap, not a parsing one,
      and not counted in any number this report produced. Second: the
      report's own effort estimate for `display: inline-block`/`inline-
      flex` was also wrong — see the new item just below.
      UPDATE: the cascade importance gap is closed by Q6 (ea2eba6).

- [x] `display: flow-root` (a4852af) now maps to `Display::Block` — same scoped
      simplification `table-caption` already uses (no real "establishes
      its own block formatting context" behavior, the one thing that
      actually distinguishes it from plain `block`, mainly used to
      contain floats without the old `overflow: hidden` hack) — a real
      block-level box, in its source position, just without the
      float-containment behavior. 1 new permanent test, using a `<span>`
      (not a `<div>`, which already defaults to `display: block` via
      `default.css` and so would report identical layout whether this
      worked or not) to confirm `flow-root` actually gets its own block
      box rather than being flattened into the surrounding inline run.
      Only 4 of the CSS-gap report's 128 `display` failures on the
      Wikipedia Main Page were `flow-root` — the bulk (112) is `inline-
      block`/`inline-flex`, deliberately *not* attempted here, see the
      next item.

- [x] **`display: inline-block`/`inline-flex`** (244a8f4) — the CSS-gap report's
      single biggest corrected finding (`inline-block`, 72 occurrences on
      the Wikipedia Main Page, and `inline-flex`, 40, together more than
      the rest of that report's entire `display` breakdown combined) now
      really works, not just parses. Confirmed by reading `collect_
      children` directly before starting: an element whose own `display`
      wasn't `Block`/`Table`/`Flex`/`Grid` was never given its own box at
      all — it was flattened into the shared inline run, and `InlineItem`
      only had `Text`/`Image` variants, nowhere for an arbitrary boxed
      element with its own children to go.

      A new `InlineItem::Embedded(Box<LayoutBox>)` variant carries the
      *unmeasured* element (built via the same `build_box_for_element`
      the block-level path already calls) into the run with no flush of
      pending content — `collect_children` gets one new dispatch arm for
      `Display::InlineBlock`/`InlineFlex`, inserted between the existing
      block-level and flatten-and-recurse arms. `inline-block`'s own
      content-building needed no new code at all (it already falls
      through every `Table`/`Flex`/`Grid` special case into the generic
      `Blocks` path `Block` already uses); `inline-flex` needed exactly
      two one-line `matches!` widenings (`build_box_for_element`,
      `measure`) on what were previously exact `== Display::Flex`
      checks — confirmed by grep that nothing inside `measure_flex`/
      `measure_flex_row`/`measure_flex_column` itself reads `Display::
      Flex` at all, so those two widenings are the entire flex-specific
      footprint this needed.

      `measure_mixed_inline` does the real work: resolves `width: auto`
      via `cell_natural_width`'s own shrink-to-fit (reusing `float`'s
      exact clamp formula in real mode; a textbook CSS2.1 §10.3.5 form
      in `cell_natural_width`'s own sizing-only trial calls instead,
      since the float formula degenerates incorrectly at the `content_w
      == 0.0` extreme those probes actually use), then calls the real
      `measure()` at a placeholder position, repositioning the result
      with `shift_laid_tree` once pass 2 knows the item's real line
      position — the same provisional-then-shift trick float's own
      positioning already uses. A new `pending: &mut Vec<
      PendingPositioned>` parameter lets a `position: absolute`
      descendant *inside* a `position: static` embedded box keep
      bubbling out to its real containing block, exactly like `measure`'s
      own `Blocks` arm already does (this function never needed it
      before since its only prior non-text item, `Image`, is a void
      element with no descendants). `MixedPositioned::Image` was renamed
      to `::Embedded` (a mechanical rename across every site that
      matches it — paint, hit-test, the scroll-shift path, `mixed_
      inline_extent`, a few test helpers — none of which actually needed
      new logic, since all of them already recursed/dispatched generically
      on the `Laid` they held, confirmed by their own existing doc
      comments before this landed).

      5 new permanent tests, including a real regression: live testing
      against the Wikipedia Main Page (not any of the first 4 tests)
      caught a genuine panic `cargo test` never would have — `measure_
      mixed_inline` always runs *both* its passes regardless of `trial`,
      so an `Embedded` item's pass-2 handling unconditionally assumed a
      real `Laid` existed, which is false for `cell_natural_width`'s own
      two sizing-only probes (real CSS auto table-layout column sizing,
      confirmed as the actual real-world trigger). Fixed by storing each
      item's own resolved style alongside `laid: Option<Laid>` and
      branching on whether a real measurement happened, with a 5th test
      (an inline-block inside a `<table>` cell) added specifically to
      keep this one from regressing silently.

      Live-verified against the real Wikipedia Main Page through a real
      `wire` daemon: zero `display: inline-block`/`inline-flex` lines
      remain in `SCRATCHY_CSS_DEBUG=1`'s own output (down from 112
      combined), zero panics, confirmed via a real `grim` screenshot
      that the page's own page-tools row (Article/Talk/Read/Edit/View
      history) now renders as a coherent horizontal strip instead of
      whatever document-order stacking it fell back to before.

      Deliberately not attempted, named precisely rather than silently
      folded in: `inline-grid` (would need the identical two-`matches!`-
      widening treatment applied to the `Grid` guards instead of `Flex`,
      a trivial separate follow-on); any `vertical-align` beyond the
      same bottom-alignment approximation inline `<img>` already has (no
      real baseline is threaded out of cosmic-text anywhere in this
      file); `%` width/height on an embedded box against an indefinite
      containing block (the same pre-existing gap every other property
      already has); perf/recursion-depth tuning for inline-block nested
      inside inline-block (works correctly today via plain recursion,
      just not optimized — one more real recursive `measure()` call per
      nesting level, same budget-participation every other nested box
      already gets, no new mechanism).

- [x] `pt` unit (17dd346, CSS-gap report: 64 `font-size` + 20 `line-height`
      occurrences on the Wikipedia Main Page) now parses wherever `px`/
      `em`/`rem` already do — `parse_length`, `parse_length_percent`,
      and `line-height`'s own dedicated `parse_line_height` grammar
      (confirmed by grep to be the only 3 places that match a `px`
      dimension token in this file, so this is the complete fix, not a
      partial one). Converted straight to `Length::Px` at parse time,
      using CSS's own exact fixed ratio (1in = 96px, 1pt = 1in/72), not
      a new `Length` variant — avoids teaching every consumer of that
      enum (`resolve`, `BoxLength`, the grid/flex track machinery, ...)
      a fourth absolute-unit case identical to `Px` everywhere it
      matters. 3 new permanent tests (a box length, `font-size`, and
      `line-height`, each checked against the exact expected pixel
      value, not just "didn't fail to parse").

- [x] `white-space: nowrap` (CSS-gap report: 42 occurrences — found via
      live testing against a real Wikipedia page, not the gap report
      directly: the Vector skin's own top-of-page tabs — `Article`/
      `Talk`, `Read`/`Edit`/`View history` — were wrapping mid-word
      ("Arti"/"cle" on separate lines) inside their own narrow nav
      boxes, since nowrap was silently dropped and ordinary wrapping
      kicked in instead). Added `WhiteSpace::Nowrap`, parsed the same
      way `pre` already is; the plain-text fast path's `shape_spans`
      (its `pre: bool` param, despite the name, now really means
      "disable wrapping" — both `Pre` and `Nowrap` pass `true` into
      cosmic-text's `Wrap::None`) initially needed to change — later
      extended to `measure_mixed_inline`'s own greedy wrapper too (the
      path a run containing an inline `<img>`/inline-block takes
      instead), see the "flex automatic minimum size" item below, since
      that fix's own correctness depends on a `nowrap` item's min-
      content matching its max-content exactly, which needs this too.
      2 new permanent tests (nowrap keeps a long run on one line in a
      box far narrower than the text; sanity-checked against the same
      markup without nowrap, which should still wrap onto several
      lines).

      **Found during the same live-testing pass, not fixed by this
      item**: the small black-square placeholders next to the Wikipedia
      logo/personal-tools dropdown are `mask-image` icons (see that
      item's own entry — deliberately deprioritized, not a new finding).
      (A second finding from this same pass — the nav tabs visibly
      overlapping once wrapping stopped silently absorbing the overflow
      — is now its own fixed item, just below.)

- [x] **Flex items' "automatic minimum size" (CSS Flexbox §4.5) —
      found live, same Wikipedia nav-tabs pass as `white-space: nowrap`
      above.** With that fix landed, the tabs stopped wrapping — but
      then visibly overlapped each other instead: each `<li>` (`Article`/
      `Talk`, `Read`/`Edit`/`View history`) is an auto-width flex item
      with no explicit `min-width`, competing for space with its
      siblings in a row narrower than their combined natural width. This
      engine defaulted every such item's shrink floor to a hardcoded
      `0`, so the shrink phase squeezed each one's box narrower than its
      own unbreakable `nowrap` text, overflowing it past the box's own
      edge into the next item instead of the item simply not fully
      fitting the row (real CSS's own behavior for content that can't
      be made to fit) — real CSS's own automatic minimum size is the
      item's content-based minimum (`cell_natural_width`'s own `min`
      probe), not `0`, when `min-width` isn't declared. Fixed in both
      `measure_flex_row`'s own `Prep.min` (clamped to the item's own
      `basis`, so an explicit `width`/`flex-basis` narrower than its
      min-content isn't forced wider) — `measure_flex_column`'s
      `min_height` default is a separate, more involved question (the
      block axis's own automatic minimum depends on the item's actual
      layout result, not just `cell_natural_width`) and is left as its
      own, not-yet-attempted gap, since it wasn't what broke here.

      This also surfaced a second, smaller bug in the same investigation:
      `cell_natural_width`'s own min/max-content probing always goes
      through `measure_mixed_inline` (see that function's own docs), so
      a `nowrap` item's *natural width* was wrong too before this item's
      own `white-space: nowrap` fix was extended there (a multi-word
      `nowrap` run's "min" probe would otherwise still wrap at every
      space, understating its real, unbreakable minimum) — see that
      item's own updated note.

      2 new permanent tests: a flex row narrower than two auto-width
      `nowrap` items' combined natural width keeps both at their full
      width (overflowing the row, not overlapping each other); a mixed
      run (text + inline `<img>`) with `white-space: nowrap` stays on
      one line, the same guarantee the plain-text fast path already had
      before this item, verifying the `measure_mixed_inline` part of the
      fix directly.

- [x] (312a345) **`mask-image`/`-webkit-mask-image` (icon alpha-masking) — real
      numbers in hand, deliberately sent to the bottom of the priority
      list, the user's own explicit call.** The CSS-gap report's single
      largest raw count by far (436 occurrences on the Wikipedia Main
      Page, every one declared twice — standard plus `-webkit-` prefix —
      for the same underlying icons: edit pencil, star, bookmark,
      hamburger menu). A wholly new rendering primitive, not a parsing
      gap: real CSS `mask-image` clips a box's own painted output by
      another image's alpha channel (here, almost always a small SVG/
      data-URI glyph used as a stencil over a solid `background-color`
      fill — Wikimedia's own icon system). Nothing in this codebase
      decodes a mask and applies it as an alpha multiply over an
      existing paint — would need its own decode path (reusing `decode_
      data_uri`/`decode_image_bytes`, already built for `<img>`/
      `background-image`) plus a new per-pixel composite step in `paint`.
      Deliberately deprioritized despite the raw count: every real use
      found here is a small UI icon glyph, not core content — missing
      one leaves a blank space where an icon would be, not a broken
      layout, unlike `vertical-align`/`background`/per-side borders/etc.

- [ ] **JavaScript — engine choice (split into QUEUE Tier 7, Q33-0..Q33g,
      on 2026-11-14), investigated, not started; last
      pillar per the user's own explicit ordering.** Recommendation:
      `boa_engine` (crates.io) — the only realistic option that keeps
      scratchy's current all-Rust build with zero new C toolchain
      dependency. Compared against `rquickjs` (QuickJS C FFI bindings)
      and `rusty_v8`/`deno_core` (real V8): neither alternative ships
      any DOM/web API surface either (confirmed — both are explicit
      about scope: "bring your own bindings"), so engine choice doesn't
      reduce the real work here at all, which is writing scratchy's own
      `getElementById`/`querySelector`/`textContent`/`classList`/
      `setAttribute`/`addEventListener`/`console.log` surface as native
      Rust closures over the real `Handle`/`NodeData` tree regardless of
      which engine sits underneath. `boa_engine`'s embedding API
      (`NativeFunction`/`JsData`/`NativeObject`) registers a Rust closure
      as a JS-callable global with no code-generation step, the same
      shape `boa_runtime`'s own `console` example already uses — a
      direct match for a minimal DOM surface. ~96% Test262 conformance
      per one third-party benchmark (closures/classes/template literals/
      basic Promises all covered — what a typical `<script>` block
      needs), slowest of the three on raw JS throughput, which doesn't
      matter here since top-tier perf was never a stated requirement.
      `rquickjs` is the fallback if `boa`'s completeness turns out
      insufficient later, at the cost of a first-ever C build step for
      this project; V8/`deno_core` was ruled out outright — its speed/
      conformance edge solves a problem scratchy doesn't have, at far
      higher embedding and build complexity.

      **DECISION (user, 2026-11-15, "Go with rquickjs"): the engine is
      rquickjs 0.14.0 (QuickJS), NOT boa. Landed in Q33a. The boa
      recommendation above is superseded.**

      **Q33-0 spike measurements (2026-11-15, loaded 8-core box, so wall
      times are noisy; CPU time is the steadier number).** Throwaway
      crates in the scratchpad (not committed), release profile, stub
      `window/document/console` globals, scripts wrapped in a function
      (parse-only, then called). Corpus: moment 176KB, d3 280KB, lodash
      544KB, three.js 670KB (1.67MB total; jquery/react-dom need a real
      DOM/React so they threw in both engines and are not counted).
        metric                                 boa_engine 0.22 | rquickjs 0.14
        parse+exec, 1.67MB                     ~1050ms         | ~150ms (7x)
        three.min.js 670KB                     440ms           | 73ms
        CPU loop (fib25, 200k obj sort, JSON)  1.1s            | 0.47s (2.3x)
        runtime init                           ~0.9ms          | ~0.4ms
        RSS, engine idle                       9MB             | 3.7MB
        RSS after 1.9MB JS                     126MB           | 55MB
        clean build, engine alone              3m43 / 21m CPU  | 50s / 57s CPU
        stripped binary, engine + stub         13.0MB          | 1.66MB
      scratchy today: clean release build 5m11 wall / 37m CPU, binary
      13.5MB (11.1MB stripped). Adding boa is therefore roughly +60% CPU
      build time and about +13MB (binary roughly doubles; boa's ICU data
      dominates); rquickjs is +~1 minute and +~1.3MB. rquickjs-sys
      compiles the bundled QuickJS with `cc`; no libclang/bindgen was
      needed here (gcc only). Verdict: no "several seconds" blocker for
      boa, but QuickJS is cheaper on every measured axis. Open cost:
      QuickJS is C (unsafe FFI, lifetime ergonomics around `Handle`
      wrappers); boa is safe Rust with a friendlier embedding for
      Rc-based trees.

      `App::document` is already a live, persistent `Handle` (`Rc<Node>`
      from `markup5ever_rcdom`) that gets rebuilt into a fresh layout
      tree on every live change already (resize, `@media`, link-nav —
      `App::redraw` already does a full `build_layout_tree` rebuild, not
      an incremental diff) — so a JS engine mutating that same real DOM
      and marking layout dirty can reuse that existing rebuild pipeline
      outright, no new "virtual DOM" or diffing layer needed. The real
      gap: `markup5ever_rcdom` is purely html5ever's *parse*-target
      structure, with no `createElement`/`appendChild`/`setAttribute`
      API of its own — DOM-mutation methods are new Rust code no matter
      which engine is chosen.


- [x] **Vim-inspired keybindings + a `:` command bar** (the plan recorded
      here, now built — `d762dbc`, `5c085bd` (v69), `cf35f56` (v70),
      `73ee636`/`7be36af`/`ad175f6`/`051cb28`,
      `scratchy/history-nav-refresh`). The design questions this plan
      worked through before anything was built — not a separate crate,
      JS config deferred until the JS pillar lands, the explicit
      `:set vim`/`:set novim` toggle over bare-key-always-on or a
      tmux-style prefix, the `SIGUSR1` out-of-band bootstrap for turning
      vim mode back on once it's off, `:` left as an unguarded bare key —
      were each resolved exactly as decided here, not revisited.

      Landed: `VimMode`/`Action` enums and `resolve_binding(key, mods,
      mode) -> Option<Action>` as the one hardcoded chokepoint a future
      scriptable `bind()` would call into instead of requiring a
      redesign; `j`/`k` scroll, `Ctrl-d`/`Ctrl-u` half-page, `:` enters
      command mode (all pre-existing); a real navigation history stack
      (`App::history`, seeded with the opening page) with `h`/`l` bound
      to `Action::HistoryBack`/`HistoryFront` (a silent no-op at either
      end of the stack, key-repeat ignored so holding the key doesn't
      blow through several entries before one fetch lands); the `:`
      command bar itself, painted as a bottom chrome strip
      (`paint_command_bar`, settling the "where does it paint" question
      this plan left open) with real text editing, Tab completion/
      cycling over `COMMAND_NAMES`, and clipboard paste; `:go <url>`
      (renamed from `:visit`), `:historyBack`/`:historyFront`/`:refresh`
      (falling back to `base_url` when history is empty, so refreshing
      the very first page works too), `:set vim`/`:set novim`, `:help`
      (`:q` from the help page goes back to the page you were on, not a
      full quit), `:q`/`:quit`; the real `SIGUSR1` bootstrap
      (`signal-hook`'s `Signals` iterator on its own background thread,
      `AppEvent::ToggleVimMode` into the existing `EventLoopProxy`
      channel — not a raw signal handler touching `App` state directly).
      Also landed in the same pass, not originally part of this plan but
      the same PRs: `scratchy <url>` (or `--socket <path> <url>`) now
      fetches a page live through `wire` directly — `classify_input`
      disambiguates an absolute `http`/`https` URL from a local path —
      so `snag url | scratchy` is no longer the only way to open a live
      page (`snag` is still the only thing that bundles a page to a
      single self-contained file up front).

      **Still fully open, as originally scoped**: link hints (Vimium/
      qutebrowser's label-and-type-to-follow) — not proposed for v1,
      `resolve_binding`'s own `mode` parameter is already shaped to grow
      a third mode for it without a redesign; the rest of real vim's
      motion vocabulary beyond `j`/`k`/`Ctrl-d`/`Ctrl-u` (`gg`/`G`/
      counts/etc.); a JS-scriptable `bind()` config layer (deferred
      until the JS pillar lands, specifically so `boa_engine` is only
      ever embedded once); the automatic half of Vimium-style vim-mode
      suspension (auto-suspend on a focused `<input>` — scratchy has no
      forms/focus yet, so only the manual `:set novim` toggle is
      buildable today).

- [x] **Move page shaping off the main thread** (the 4-step sequencing
      plan recorded here, now built — `968df78`, `81fb1cc`, `b5184e4`,
      `a58e2b0`). Steps 1–3 landed as planned: `Rc<DecodedImage>` ->
      `Arc<DecodedImage>` everywhere; `layout_generation` bumped on every
      resize/`@media`/navigation/streaming-chunk, threaded into every
      background-shaping request and checked against on arrival so a
      stale result from a since-superseded reshape is silently dropped;
      `measure_document` now runs on its own background thread with its
      own independent `FontSystem`/`SwashCache` pair, isolated behind
      `catch_unwind` so a panic on one pathological page can't
      permanently break every navigation after it.

      Step 4 (revisit `PROGRESSIVE_LAYOUT_TICK_BUDGET` once step 3
      landed) surfaced a real problem real-world testing against a live
      Wikipedia page found in the first one-shot-thread-per-rebuild
      version of step 3, and both of this plan's own open questions got
      resolved in the fix, not left open: a *persistent* background
      worker (`spawn_shaping_worker`, started once at construction, not
      spawned fresh per rebuild — avoids re-paying `new_font_system()`'s
      system-font-database scan on every navigation) that still ticks
      internally at roughly the same budget and still delivers progress
      per tick (`AppEvent::ShapeResult`), so a big page keeps filling in
      progressively on the main thread exactly as before this plan
      started, despite shaping now happening on a different thread
      entirely — chosen over one unticked call to completion, which
      turned "laggy while loading" into "blank for most of a minute" on
      a big real page when tried.

      **Explicitly NOT fixed by this plan, exactly as flagged when it
      was written**:

      - [ ] `build_layout_tree_with_resources`'s own DOM-walk-and-
            cascade-match cost is still a fully synchronous, unbudgeted,
            main-thread-blocking call — `markup5ever_rcdom`'s own
            `Handle = Rc<Node>` is categorically `!Send`, so it can never
            leave the main thread without a from-scratch, thread-safe DOM
            representation (out of scope, not attempted). A big page's
            very first load can still show the same "laggy while
            loading" symptom from this one step alone, even though
            `measure_document` itself is now fully backgrounded.

- [x] **(Done, `f7dfda9`.) User-reported, investigated 2026-11-11, base
      `5f8ceed`): whitespace-only text between block-level siblings
      becomes its own full-height (~18-20px) anonymous line box.**
      **Landed** (`f7dfda9`): `discard_if_collapsible_
      whitespace_only`, called at both flush points. It matches the
      exact `" "` shape non-`pre` whitespace-only text nodes produce, so
      `pre` whitespace and runs containing an image, inline-block or
      list marker are kept. SoHo Weekly News page: title y=636 -> 156,
      document 20336px -> 14227px, 0 whitespace-only text boxes left.
      Tests: `whitespace_between_block_siblings_generates_no_line_box`
      (fails without the fix) and `white_space_pre_keeps_whitespace_
      only_lines_between_block_siblings`. Known miss, documented on
      the function: a `pre` text node that is exactly one space is
      dropped. Leading/trailing-space trimming within real lines (the
      "nice-to-have" below) was not attempted.
      Original investigation notes follow. This
      is the cause of the huge blank band at the top of real Wikipedia
      articles (repro: `scratchy https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/
      SoHo_Weekly_News --socket $(wire --print-socket)`, or `snag` that
      URL to a file). Measured with a throwaway `Laid`-tree dump at
      1050px viewport width: 85 whitespace-only `LaidContent::Text`
      boxes (text `" "` or `"  "`) in the first 1200px alone. Every box
      between the header (ends at y=228) and the article title (y=636)
      is either one of these or an otherwise-empty container whose only
      height comes from them (the empty-looking sticky-header/titlebar
      wrappers are `Blocks(1)` around a single `"  "` line). The light-
      grey bands above and below the header in the user's screenshot
      are the same thing: whitespace lines outside the white page
      container showing the `<html>` background through.

      **Root cause**: `collect_children`'s `NodeData::Text` arm runs
      `normalize_whitespace` (which turns `"\n    "` into `""`) and then
      unconditionally does `text.push(' ')`, so every inter-tag
      newline/indent becomes a `" "` `InlineItem::Text`. The two flush
      points (before a block-level child in `collect_children`, and the
      trailing flush at the end of `build_children_with_prefix`) then
      wrap whatever is in `pending` in an anonymous `BoxContent::Inline`
      box, even when it holds nothing but whitespace. `measure` shapes
      that as one real line. This was already a known hazard: NOTES.md
      (the v56 stacking-order writeup) and several tests (e.g. the
      comments near the font-family test, "Deliberately no whitespace
      between the `<p>`s") work around it by writing fixtures with no
      inter-tag whitespace. It was never filed as a bug, though.

      **Spec**: CSS 2.1 §9.2.2.1: "White space content that would
      subsequently be collapsed away according to the 'white-space'
      property does not generate any anonymous inline boxes." See also
      §16.6.1, phase II: spaces at the start/end of a line are removed,
      so a line that is only collapsible whitespace has no content. Per
      §10.8, line boxes with no content are treated as zero-height.

      **Verified fix direction** (throwaway patch, reverted, not
      committed): at both flush points, if every item in `pending` is an
      `InlineItem::Text` whose text `.trim().is_empty()`, discard
      `pending` instead of emitting a box. On the SoHo page this alone
      moved the article title from y=636 to y=156 and shrank the
      document from 20336px to 14227px (~30% of the page's total height
      was whitespace lines). It also removed both grey bands. Things to
      get right in the real fix:
      - Only discard when collapsible: `WhiteSpace::Pre` keeps its text
        verbatim (that `NodeData::Text` arm already special-cases it),
        so a whitespace-only `pre` run must still produce its line(s).
        `Nowrap` collapses whitespace like `Normal`, so discard it
        there too.
      - Never discard a run that also contains an `InlineItem::Image`
        or `InlineItem::Embedded`. Those are real content, and the
        whitespace next to them is meaningful inter-word space.
      - Consider the related flex/grid child-building paths
        (`build_flex_children`/`build_table_children`/grid), which
        should already skip text nodes. Confirm that with the dump, and
        don't assume it.
      - A list-marker `prefix` (`build_children_with_prefix`'s `<li>`
        bullet) is real content. An `<li>` whose only text is whitespace
        must keep its marker line.
      - Nice-to-have, not required: leading whitespace at the start of a
        real line and trailing whitespace at its end (§16.6.1 phase II;
        e.g. `"Jump to content  "` currently carries two trailing
        spaces). Only pursue it if it changes measured geometry.
      - Add a **permanent** regression test that uses a fixture with
        real newlines/indentation between block siblings (e.g.
        `"<div>\n  <p>A</p>\n  <p>B</p>\n</div>"`) and asserts the
        second `<p>`'s `y` equals the first's `y + h` (no 20px gap), plus
        a `white-space: pre` counter-case that still produces a line.
        Existing tests that deliberately strip inter-tag whitespace can
        stay as they are. Don't rewrite them.
      - Verify against the real SoHo Weekly News page: dump the `Laid`
        tree (or grim-screenshot it) and confirm the title sits right
        below the header with no grey bands.

- [x] (66bdc60) **Parse `:focus`/`:hover`/`:active`/`:focus-visible`/`:focus-
      within` as never-matching pseudo-classes, instead of rejecting
      them** (investigated 2026-11-11 on the same SoHo Weekly News page,
      base `5f8ceed`). This is a narrower, cheap first step toward the
      older `:hover`/`:focus`/`:active` item above. Today
      `OurPseudoClass` only knows `:link`/`:visited`/`:root`, so any
      selector mentioning `:focus` fails to parse and **the whole rule
      is dropped**, including `:not(:focus)` rules that should *match*
      on a page with nothing focused. Real hit: Wikipedia's
      `.mw-jump-link:not(:focus){display:block;position:absolute
      !important;clip:rect(1px,1px,1px,1px);width:1px;height:1px;
      margin:-1px;overflow:hidden;...}` is dropped, so "Jump to content"
      renders as a visible in-flow line at the top of every Wikipedia
      page instead of being taken out of flow as a 1x1 box. Fix: add
      variants for these to `OurPseudoClass` (parse + `to_css`), and
      have `match_non_ts_pseudo_class` return `false` for them. That is
      exactly true for a browser with no focus/hover state, and it makes
      `:not(:focus)` correctly match everything. Leave real state
      tracking to the older item. Test: a `:not(:focus)` rule applies
      and a plain `:hover` rule doesn't, and neither breaks parsing of
      the other selectors in the same comma-separated list (check how
      selector-list parse failure currently behaves first; a list with
      one bad selector drops the whole rule per spec, which is another
      reason to stop rejecting these). Note: `clip:` is not supported,
      but `width/height:1px` + `overflow:hidden` + `position:absolute`
      should already hide the text in practice. Verify by rendering.
